117:             return Err(FsError::InvalidPath);
118:         }
119:         let mut current = if path.starts_with('/') {
120:             ROOT_NODE
121:         } else {
122:             cwd
123:         };
124:         let start = self.node(current)?;
125:         if start.kind != EntryKind::Directory {
126:             return Err(FsError::NotDirectory);
127:         }
128: 
129:         for component in path.split('/') {
130:             if component.is_empty() {
131:                 continue;
132:             }
133:             if self.node(current)?.kind != EntryKind::Directory {
134:                 return Err(FsError::NotDirectory);
135:             }
136:             match component {
137:                 "." => {}
138:                 ".." => current = self.node(current)?.parent,
139:                 name => {
140:                     current = self.find_child(current, name).ok_or(FsError::NotFound)?;
141:                 }
142:             }
143:         }
144:         Ok(current)
145:     }
146: 
147:     pub fn create_dir(&mut self, parent: usize, name: &str) -> Result<usize, FsError> {
148:         self.create_node(parent, name, EntryKind::Directory)
149:     }
150: 
151:     pub fn create_file(&mut self, parent: usize, name: &str) -> Result<usize, FsError> {
152:         self.create_node(parent, name, EntryKind::File)
153:     }
154: 
155:     pub fn create_dir_path(&mut self, cwd: usize, path: &str) -> Result<usize, FsError> {
156:         let (parent_path, name) = split_parent(path)?;
157:         let parent = self.resolve(cwd, parent_path)?;
158:         self.create_dir(parent, name)
159:     }
160: 
161:     pub fn create_file_path(&mut self, cwd: usize, path: &str) -> Result<usize, FsError> {
162:         let (parent_path, name) = split_parent(path)?;
163:         let parent = self.resolve(cwd, parent_path)?;
164:         self.create_file(parent, name)
165:     }
166: 
167:     /// Return an existing file node or create it if its path does not exist.
168:     pub fn open_or_create_file(&mut self, cwd: usize, path: &str) -> Result<usize, FsError> {
169:         match self.resolve(cwd, path) {
170:             Ok(node) => {
171:                 if self.node(node)?.kind == EntryKind::Directory {
172:                     Err(FsError::IsDirectory)
173:                 } else {
174:                     Ok(node)
175:                 }
176:             }
177:             Err(FsError::NotFound) => self.create_file_path(cwd, path),
178:             Err(error) => Err(error),
179:         }
180:     }
181: 
182:     pub fn kind(&self, node: usize) -> Result<EntryKind, FsError> {
183:         Ok(self.node(node)?.kind)
184:     }
185: 
186:     pub fn read_file(&self, node: usize) -> Result<&[u8], FsError> {
187:         let node = self.node(node)?;
188:         if node.kind == EntryKind::Directory {
189:             return Err(FsError::IsDirectory);
190:         }
191:         Ok(&node.data[..node.data_len])
192:     }
193: 
194:     pub fn write_file(&mut self, node: usize, contents: &[u8]) -> Result<(), FsError> {
195:         if contents.len() > FILE_CAPACITY {
196:             return Err(FsError::FileTooLarge);
197:         }
198:         let node = self.node_mut(node)?;
199:         if node.kind == EntryKind::Directory {
200:             return Err(FsError::IsDirectory);
201:         }
202:         node.data[..contents.len()].copy_from_slice(contents);
203:         node.data[contents.len()..].fill(0);
204:         node.data_len = contents.len();
205:         Ok(())
206:     }
207: 
208:     pub fn write_file_path(
209:         &mut self,
210:         cwd: usize,
211:         path: &str,
212:         contents: &[u8],
213:     ) -> Result<(), FsError> {
214:         if contents.len() > FILE_CAPACITY {
215:             return Err(FsError::FileTooLarge);
216:         }
217:         let node = self.open_or_create_file(cwd, path)?;
218:         self.write_file(node, contents)
219:     }
220: 
221:     pub fn append_file(&mut self, node: usize, contents: &[u8]) -> Result<(), FsError> {
222:         let node = self.node_mut(node)?;
223:         if node.kind == EntryKind::Directory {
224:             return Err(FsError::IsDirectory);
225:         }
226:         let new_len = node
227:             .data_len
228:             .checked_add(contents.len())
229:             .filter(|length| *length <= FILE_CAPACITY)
230:             .ok_or(FsError::FileTooLarge)?;
231:         node.data[node.data_len..new_len].copy_from_slice(contents);
232:         node.data_len = new_len;
233:         Ok(())
234:     }
235: 
236:     pub fn append_file_path(
237:         &mut self,
238:         cwd: usize,
239:         path: &str,
240:         contents: &[u8],
241:     ) -> Result<(), FsError> {
242:         if contents.len() > FILE_CAPACITY {
243:             return Err(FsError::FileTooLarge);
244:         }
245:         let node = self.open_or_create_file(cwd, path)?;
246:         self.append_file(node, contents)
247:     }
248: 
249:     /// Copy a file to a destination path, replacing an existing destination file.
250:     pub fn copy_file_path(
251:         &mut self,
252:         cwd: usize,
253:         source_path: &str,
254:         destination_path: &str,
255:     ) -> Result<(), FsError> {
256:         let source = self.resolve(cwd, source_path)?;
257:         let mut contents = [0u8; FILE_CAPACITY];
258:         let length = {
259:             let source_contents = self.read_file(source)?;
260:             contents[..source_contents.len()].copy_from_slice(source_contents);
261:             source_contents.len()
262:         };
263:         let destination = self.open_or_create_file(cwd, destination_path)?;
264:         self.write_file(destination, &contents[..length])
265:     }
266: 
267:     /// Move or rename a file. Existing destinations are never overwritten.
268:     pub fn move_file_path(
269:         &mut self,
270:         cwd: usize,
271:         source_path: &str,
272:         destination_path: &str,
273:     ) -> Result<(), FsError> {
274:         let source = self.resolve(cwd, source_path)?;
275:         if source == ROOT_NODE {
276:             return Err(FsError::RootOperation);
277:         }
278:         if self.kind(source)? == EntryKind::Directory {
279:             return Err(FsError::IsDirectory);
280:         }
281: 
282:         let (parent_path, name) = split_parent(destination_path)?;
283:         validate_name(name)?;
284:         let parent = self.resolve(cwd, parent_path)?;
285:         if self.node(parent)?.kind != EntryKind::Directory {
286:             return Err(FsError::NotDirectory);
287:         }
288:         if let Some(existing) = self.find_child(parent, name) {
289:             return if existing == source {
290:                 Ok(())
291:             } else {
292:                 Err(FsError::AlreadyExists)
293:             };
294:         }
295: 
296:         let node = self.node_mut(source)?;
297:         node.parent = parent;
298:         node.name.fill(0);
299:         node.name[..name.len()].copy_from_slice(name.as_bytes());
300:         node.name_len = name.len() as u8;
301:         Ok(())
302:     }
303: 
304:     pub fn remove_file(&mut self, node: usize) -> Result<(), FsError> {
305:         if node == ROOT_NODE {
306:             return Err(FsError::RootOperation);
307:         }
308:         let entry = self.node(node)?;
309:         if entry.kind == EntryKind::Directory {
310:             return Err(FsError::IsDirectory);
311:         }
312:         self.nodes[node] = Node::EMPTY;
313:         Ok(())
314:     }
315: 
316:     pub fn remove_dir(&mut self, node: usize) -> Result<(), FsError> {
317:         if node == ROOT_NODE {
318:             return Err(FsError::RootOperation);
319:         }
320:         let entry = self.node(node)?;
321:         if entry.kind != EntryKind::Directory {
322:             return Err(FsError::NotDirectory);
323:         }
324:         if self
325:             .nodes
326:             .iter()
327:             .any(|child| child.used && child.parent == node)
328:         {
329:             return Err(FsError::DirectoryNotEmpty);
330:         }
331:         self.nodes[node] = Node::EMPTY;
332:         Ok(())
333:     }
334: 
335:     pub fn remove_file_path(&mut self, cwd: usize, path: &str) -> Result<(), FsError> {
336:         let node = self.resolve(cwd, path)?;
337:         self.remove_file(node)
338:     }
339: 
340:     pub fn remove_dir_path(&mut self, cwd: usize, path: &str) -> Result<(), FsError> {
341:         let node = self.resolve(cwd, path)?;
342:         self.remove_dir(node)
343:     }
344: 
345:     pub fn entry(&self, node: usize) -> Result<Entry<'_>, FsError> {
346:         let node = self.node(node)?;
347:         Ok(Entry {
348:             name: node.name(),
349:             kind: node.kind,
350:             size: node.data_len,
351:         })
352:     }
353: 
354:     pub fn child_count(&self, directory: usize) -> Result<usize, FsError> {
355:         if self.node(directory)?.kind != EntryKind::Directory {
356:             return Err(FsError::NotDirectory);
357:         }
358:         Ok(self
359:             .nodes
360:             .iter()
361:             .filter(|node| {
362:                 node.used
363:                     && node.parent == directory
364:                     && !(directory == ROOT_NODE && node.name_len == 0)
365:             })
366:             .count())
367:     }
368: 
369:     pub fn child_at(&self, directory: usize, ordinal: usize) -> Option<Entry<'_>> {
370:         if self.node(directory).ok()?.kind != EntryKind::Directory {
371:             return None;
372:         }
373:         self.nodes
374:             .iter()
375:             .filter(|node| {
376:                 node.used
377:                     && node.parent == directory
378:                     && !(directory == ROOT_NODE && node.name_len == 0)
379:             })
380:             .nth(ordinal)
381:             .map(|node| Entry {
382:                 name: node.name(),
383:                 kind: node.kind,
384:                 size: node.data_len,
385:             })
386:     }
387: 
388:     /// Write an absolute path into caller-provided storage and return its length.
389:     pub fn write_path(&self, node: usize, output: &mut [u8]) -> Result<usize, FsError> {
390:         self.node(node)?;
391:         let mut ancestors = [ROOT_NODE; MAX_NODES];
392:         let mut depth = 0;
393:         let mut current = node;
394:         while current != ROOT_NODE {
395:             if depth == MAX_NODES {
396:                 return Err(FsError::InvalidPath);
397:             }
398:             ancestors[depth] = current;
399:             depth += 1;
400:             current = self.node(current)?.parent;
401:         }
402: 
403:         let component_bytes = (0..depth)
404:             .map(|index| usize::from(self.nodes[ancestors[index]].name_len))
405:             .sum::<usize>();
406:         let required = 1 + component_bytes + depth.saturating_sub(1);
407:         if output.len() < required {
408:             return Err(FsError::BufferTooSmall);
409:         }
410: 
411:         output[0] = b'/';
412:         let mut length = 1;
413:         for index in (0..depth).rev() {
414:             if length > 1 {
415:                 output[length] = b'/';
416:                 length += 1;
417:             }
418:             let name = self.nodes[ancestors[index]].name();
419:             output[length..length + name.len()].copy_from_slice(name.as_bytes());
420:             length += name.len();
421:         }
422:         Ok(length)
423:     }
424: 
425:     fn create_node(
426:         &mut self,
427:         parent: usize,
428:         name: &str,
429:         kind: EntryKind,
430:     ) -> Result<usize, FsError> {
431:         let parent_node = self.node(parent)?;
432:         if parent_node.kind != EntryKind::Directory {
433:             return Err(FsError::NotDirectory);
434:         }
435:         validate_name(name)?;
436:         if self.find_child(parent, name).is_some() {
437:             return Err(FsError::AlreadyExists);
438:         }
439:         let index = self
440:             .nodes
441:             .iter()
442:             .position(|node| !node.used)
443:             .ok_or(FsError::NoSpace)?;
444:         let mut node = Node::EMPTY;
445:         node.used = true;
446:         node.kind = kind;
447:         node.parent = parent;
448:         node.name[..name.len()].copy_from_slice(name.as_bytes());
449:         node.name_len = name.len() as u8;
450:         self.nodes[index] = node;
451:         Ok(index)
452:     }
453: 
454:     fn find_child(&self, parent: usize, name: &str) -> Option<usize> {
455:         self.nodes.iter().position(|node| {
456:             node.used && node.parent == parent && node.name_len != 0 && node.name() == name
457:         })
458:     }
459: 
460:     fn node(&self, index: usize) -> Result<&Node, FsError> {
461:         self.nodes
462:             .get(index)
463:             .filter(|node| node.used)
464:             .ok_or(FsError::NotFound)
465:     }
466: 
467:     fn node_mut(&mut self, index: usize) -> Result<&mut Node, FsError> {
468:         self.nodes
469:             .get_mut(index)
470:             .filter(|node| node.used)
471:             .ok_or(FsError::NotFound)
472:     }
473: }
474: 
475: impl Default for RamFs {
476:     fn default() -> Self {
477:         Self::new()
478:     }
479: }
480: 
481: fn validate_name(name: &str) -> Result<(), FsError> {
482:     if name.is_empty() || name == "." || name == ".." || name.contains('/') || name.contains('\0') {
483:         return Err(FsError::InvalidName);
484:     }
485:     if name.len() > NAME_CAPACITY {
486:         return Err(FsError::NameTooLong);
487:     }
488:     Ok(())
489: }
490: 
491: fn split_parent(path: &str) -> Result<(&str, &str), FsError> {
492:     let path = path.trim_end_matches('/');
493:     if path.is_empty() {
494:         return Err(FsError::RootOperation);
495:     }
496:     match path.rsplit_once('/') {
497:         Some(("", name)) => Ok(("/", name)),
498:         Some((parent, name)) if !name.is_empty() => Ok((parent, name)),
499:         Some(_) => Err(FsError::InvalidPath),
500:         None => Ok((".", path)),
501:     }
502: }

## Assistant

_Thinking:_

Now I have the full VFS. The `resolve` trailing-slash fix: track `ends_with_sep = path.ends_with('/')`. If the resolved node is not a Directory AND path ended with '/', return NotDirectory. Where to apply: end of resolve:

```rust
let trailing_separator = path.len() > 1 && path.ends_with('/'); // "/" itself ok
let node = ...;
if trailing_separator && self.node(current)?.kind != EntryKind::Directory {
    return Err(FsError::NotDirectory);
}
```

Wait: path == "/": resolves to ROOT (Directory) → fine. path == "foo/": resolves to file foo → now error. path == "dir/" → Directory → fine. Also ".." with trailing? "foo/.." doesn't end with slash. "dir/../" ends with '/', resolves to parent (Directory) → fine.

But careful: resolve() is used for directories all over (cd, mkdir parent walks, etc.) — only the final-node-kind check changes: paths ending with '/' must name a directory. That's exactly ENOTDIR semantics. Implementation inside resolve at the end:

```rust
if path.ends_with('/') && self.node(current)?.kind != EntryKind::Directory {
    return Err(FsError::NotDirectory);
}
```
ROOT case: "/" → current=ROOT, kind=Directory → ok. Empty path already handled.

Effect on callers:
- `rm file/` → resolve → NotDirectory → error. ✓
- `cp src dst/` → open_or_create_file resolves "dst/" → NotDirectory → Err. Hmm — my fix D1 says trailing slash + file → error. And D5: dest resolving to existing directory (with or without trailing slash) → move INTO the directory. So cp/mv handle directories as destination specially:

For `copy_file_path(cwd, src, dst)`:
```rust
let destination = match self.resolve(cwd, destination_path) {
    Ok(node) if node.kind == Directory => {
        // destination is a directory: target = dir/basename(source)
        let base = split_parent(source_path)?.1;  // source's final component
        self.open_or_create_file(node, base)?   // hmm open_or_create resolves within node (cwd = node)
    }
    ... existing behavior
};
```
Careful with base name: source_path basename — split_parent(source_path) gives (parent, name) where name is the validated final component... but for source="file/", trailing-slash resolve now errors — good, source must not end in slash either (it resolves before read; source "file/" now NotDirectory error — consistent).

For `move_file_path`: same pattern: if dest resolves to existing directory → target inside it with source basename; reuse existing "no overwrite" logic by constructing destination = inside. Simplest: compute `effective_dest_parent` and `effective_dest_name`:

```rust
let source = resolve...;
// Destination may name a file path or an existing directory into which the source moves.
let (parent_path, name) = match self.resolve(cwd, destination_path) {
    Ok(node) if self.node(node)?.kind == EntryKind::Directory => {
        (destination_path, split_parent(source_path)?.1)  // parent = the directory, name = source basename
    }
    _ => split_parent(destination_path)?,
};
```
Then proceed with parent=resolve(cwd, parent_path), name=name. The existing AlreadyExists logic then handles existing target (never overwrite for mv; cp replaces via open_or_create+write).

For cp: destination dir exists → open inside dir with source basename. cp currently: `let destination = self.open_or_create_file(cwd, destination_path)?;` → change:
```rust
let destination = match self.resolve(cwd, destination_path) {
    Ok(node) if self.node(node)?.kind == EntryKind::Directory => {
        let base = split_parent(source_path)?.1;
        self.open_or_create_file(node, base)?
    }
    Ok(_) | Err(FsError::NotFound) => self.open_or_create_file(cwd, destination_path)?,
    Err(error) => return Err(error),
};
```

Node already created → empty file left. Fix: in append_file_path, if the path resolves OK to an existing node, check cumulative size BEFORE creating... but open_or_create also creates. Restructure:
```rust
pub fn append_file_path(&mut self, cwd, path, contents) -> Result<(), FsError> {
    if contents.len() > FILE_CAPACITY { return Err(FileTooLarge); }
    // Pre-check the cumulative bound so a failed append never creates or
    // partially mutates a node.
    if let Ok(node) = self.resolve(cwd, path) {
        let data_len = self.node(node)?.data_len;
        data_len.checked_add(contents.len()).filter(|l| *l <= FILE_CAPACITY).ok_or(FsError::FileTooLarge)?;
    }
    let node = self.open_or_create_file(cwd, path)?;
    self.append_file(node, contents)
}
```

10: /// Run Crabshell forever on the bootstrap CPU.
11: pub fn run(
12:     filesystem: &mut RamFs,
13:     mut cwd: usize,
14:     memory_map: &MemoryMap,
15:     frame_allocator: *const FrameAllocator,
16:     runtime: *mut (),
17: ) -> ! {
18:     crate::kprintln!("crabshell: ready (volatile RAM filesystem)");
19:     print_motd(filesystem, cwd);
20: 
21:     let mut input = [0u8; MAX_LINE_LENGTH];
22:     let mut reported_input_wait = false;
23:     loop {
24:         print_prompt(filesystem, cwd);
25:         let length = read_line(
26:             &mut input,
27:             filesystem,
28:             cwd,
29:             runtime,
30:             &mut reported_input_wait,
31:         );
32:         if length == 0 {
33:             continue;
34:         }
35: 
36:         let line = match core::str::from_utf8(&input[..length]) {
37:             Ok(line) => line,
38:             Err(_) => {
39:                 crate::kprintln!("crabshell: input must be valid UTF-8");
40:                 continue;
41:             }
42:         };
43: 
44:         match parse(line) {
45:             Ok(Some(command)) => {
46:                 execute(command, filesystem, &mut cwd, memory_map, frame_allocator)
47:             }
48:             Ok(None) => {}
49:             Err(error) => print_parse_error(error),
50:         }
51:     }
52: }

54: fn print_prompt(filesystem: &RamFs, cwd: usize) {
55:     let mut path = [0u8; MAX_PATH_CAPACITY];
56:     match filesystem.write_path(cwd, &mut path) {
57:         Ok(length) => match core::str::from_utf8(&path[..length]) {
58:             Ok(path) => serial::print(format_args!("root@rustix:{}# ", path)),
59:             Err(_) => serial::print(format_args!("root@rustix:/?# ")),
60:         },
61:         Err(_) => serial::print(format_args!("root@rustix:/?# ")),
62:     }
63: }
64: 
65: fn read_line(
66:     input: &mut [u8; MAX_LINE_LENGTH],
67:     filesystem: &RamFs,
68:     cwd: usize,
69:     runtime: *mut (),
70:     reported_input_wait: &mut bool,
71: ) -> usize {
72:     let mut length = 0;
73:     loop {
74:         let byte = match serial::try_read_byte() {
75:             Some(byte) => byte,
76:             None => {
77:                 if let Some(scancode) = crate::arch::x86_64::keyboard::try_read_irq_scancode() {
78:                     if scancode == 0xe0 || scancode & 0x80 == 0 {
79:                         serial::print(format_args!(
80:                             "\r\nPS/2 raw scancode IRQ: {:#04x}\r\n",
81:                             scancode
82:                         ));
83:                         print_prompt(filesystem, cwd);
84:                         for &byte in &input[..length] {
85:                             serial::print(format_args!("{}", byte as char));
86:                         }
87:                     }
88:                     continue;
89:                 }
90:                 if runtime.is_null() {
91:                     core::hint::spin_loop();
92:                 } else {
93:                     if !*reported_input_wait {
94:                         crate::kprintln!("kernel shell thread runtime: cooperative input wait");
95:                         *reported_input_wait = true;
96:                     }
97:                     crate::kernel_threads::wait_for_input(runtime);
98:                 }
99:                 continue;
100:             }
101:         };
102: 
103:         match byte {
104:             b'\r' | b'\n' => {
105:                 crate::kprintln!();
106:                 return length;
107:             }
108:             0x03 => {
109:                 crate::kprintln!("^C");
110:                 return 0;
111:             }
112:             0x08 | 0x7f if length > 0 => {
113:                 length -= 1;
114:                 serial::print(format_args!("\x08 \x08"));
115:             }
116:             0x20..=0x7e if length < input.len() => {
117:                 input[length] = byte;
118:                 length += 1;
119:                 serial::print(format_args!("{}", byte as char));
120:             }
121:             0x20..=0x7e => serial::print(format_args!("\x07")),
122:             _ => {}
123:         }
124:     }
125: }

241: fn list_directory(filesystem: &RamFs, directory: usize) {
242:     match filesystem.kind(directory) {
243:         Ok(EntryKind::File) => match filesystem.entry(directory) {
244:             Ok(entry) => crate::kprintln!("{} ({} bytes)", entry.name, entry.size),
245:             Err(error) => report_fs_error(error),
246:         },
247:         Err(error) => report_fs_error(error),
248:         Ok(EntryKind::Directory) => match filesystem.child_count(directory) {
249:             Ok(0) => crate::kprintln!("(empty)"),
250:             Ok(count) => {
251:                 for index in 0..count {
252:                     let Some(entry) = filesystem.child_at(directory, index) else {
253:                         crate::kprintln!("crabshell: directory changed while listing");
254:                         return;
255:                     };
256:                     match entry.kind {
257:                         EntryKind::Directory => crate::kprintln!("{}/", entry.name),
258:                         EntryKind::File => {
259:                             crate::kprintln!("{}  {} bytes", entry.name, entry.size)
260:                         }
261:                     }
262:                 }
263:             }
264:             Err(error) => report_fs_error(error),
265:         },
266:     }
267: }
268: 
269: fn print_file_contents(text: &str) {
270:     for (index, line) in text.split('\n').enumerate() {
271:         if index != 0 {
272:             serial::print(format_args!("\r\n"));
273:         }
274:         serial::print(format_args!("{}", line.strip_suffix('\r').unwrap_or(line)));
275:     }
276:     if !text.ends_with('\n') {
277:         crate::kprintln!();
278:     }
279: }
280: 
281: fn print_motd(filesystem: &RamFs, cwd: usize) {
282:     if let Ok(node) = filesystem.resolve(cwd, "/etc/motd") {
283:         if let Ok(bytes) = filesystem.read_file(node) {
284:             if let Ok(text) = core::str::from_utf8(bytes) {
285:                 print_file_contents(text);
286:             }
287:         }
288:     }
289: }
290: 
291: fn print_help() {
292:     crate::kprintln!("Crabshell commands:");
293:     crate::kprintln!("  help                 show this help");
294:     crate::kprintln!("  clear                clear an ANSI-compatible terminal");
295:     crate::kprintln!("  pwd                  print the current directory");
296:     crate::kprintln!("  meminfo              show validated memory and frame counts");
297:     crate::kprintln!("  ls [PATH]            list a directory or file");
298:     crate::kprintln!("  cd PATH              change the current directory");
299:     crate::kprintln!("  mkdir PATH            create a directory");
300:     crate::kprintln!("  touch PATH            create an empty file");
301:     crate::kprintln!("  write PATH TEXT       replace a file's contents");
302:     crate::kprintln!("  append PATH TEXT      append text to a file");
303:     crate::kprintln!("  cp SOURCE DEST        copy a file, replacing DEST if it exists");
304:     crate::kprintln!("  mv SOURCE DEST        move/rename; refuse existing DEST");
305:     crate::kprintln!("  cat PATH              print a text file");
306:     crate::kprintln!("  rm PATH               remove a file");
307:     crate::kprintln!("  rmdir PATH            remove an empty directory");
308:     crate::kprintln!("  write/append accept a quoted or unquoted text remainder.");
309:     crate::kprintln!("  RAM filesystem: 31 entries plus root; files up to 1024 bytes.");
310: }

312: fn print_parse_error(error: ParseError) {
313:     match error {
314:         ParseError::UnknownCommand => crate::kprintln!("crabshell: unknown command (try 'help')"),
315:         ParseError::MissingArgument => crate::kprintln!("crabshell: missing path argument"),
316:         ParseError::MissingText => {
317:             crate::kprintln!("crabshell: missing text (usage: write PATH TEXT)")
318:         }
319:         ParseError::TooManyArguments => crate::kprintln!("crabshell: too many arguments"),
320:         ParseError::UnterminatedQuote => crate::kprintln!("crabshell: unterminated text quote"),
321:     }
322: }
323: 
324: fn report_fs_error(error: FsError) {
325:     let message = match error {
326:         FsError::InvalidPath => "invalid path",
327:         FsError::InvalidName => "invalid file name",
328:         FsError::NameTooLong => "file name is too long (maximum 31 UTF-8 bytes)",
329:         FsError::AlreadyExists => "an entry with that name already exists",
330:         FsError::NotFound => "path not found",
331:         FsError::NotDirectory => "a path component is not a directory",
332:         FsError::IsDirectory => "operation requires a file, not a directory",
333:         FsError::DirectoryNotEmpty => "directory is not empty",
334:         FsError::NoSpace => "RAM filesystem is full",
335:         FsError::FileTooLarge => "file exceeds the 1024-byte limit",
336:         FsError::RootOperation => "operation is not permitted on the root directory",
337:         FsError::BufferTooSmall => "path exceeds the display buffer",
338:     };
339:     crate::kprintln!("crabshell: {}", message);
340: }

```rust
            }

}
```

enum AcpiSpanAuthorization {
    AcpiTableMemory,
    LegacyRsdpReserved,
}

/// A physical ACPI-related byte span whose complete 4 KiB mapping extent has
/// explicit memory-map authorization and fits the known physical-address width.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ValidatedAcpiSpan {
    byte_start: u64,
    byte_end: u64,
    mapping_start: u64,
    mapping_end: u64,
    authorization: AcpiSpanAuthorization,
}

impl ValidatedAcpiSpan {
    pub const fn byte_start(self) -> u64 {
        self.byte_start
    }

    pub const fn byte_end(self) -> u64 {
        self.byte_end
    }

    pub const fn mapping_start(self) -> u64 {
        self.mapping_start
    }

    pub const fn mapping_end(self) -> u64 {
        self.mapping_end
    }

    /// Whether one aligned 4 KiB physical page is fully covered by this span's
    /// validated page-rounded mapping extent.
    pub fn contains_mapping_page(self, physical_page: u64) -> bool {
        if physical_page & 0xfff != 0 {
            return false;
        }
        let Some(page_end) = physical_page.checked_add(4096) else {
            return false;
        };
        let authorization_contains_page = match self.authorization {
            AcpiSpanAuthorization::AcpiTableMemory => true,
            AcpiSpanAuthorization::LegacyRsdpReserved => {
                physical_page >= 0x80_000 && page_end <= 0x10_0000
            }
        };
        authorization_contains_page
            && physical_page >= self.mapping_start
            && page_end <= self.mapping_end
    }
}

/// Validated, allocation-free snapshot of the physical memory map.
#[derive(Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct MemoryMap {
    regions: [PhysicalRegion; MAX_MEMORY_REGIONS],
    region_count: usize,
    usable_bytes: u64,
}

impl MemoryMap {
    const EMPTY: Self = Self {
        regions: [PhysicalRegion::EMPTY; MAX_MEMORY_REGIONS],
        region_count: 0,
        usable_bytes: 0,
    };

    pub fn from_raw(regions: &[RawMemoryRegion]) -> Result<Self, MemoryMapError> {
        let mut builder = MemoryMapBuilder::new();
        for region in regions {
            builder.push(region.base, region.length, region.kind)?;
        }
        builder.finish()
    }

    pub fn regions(&self) -> &[PhysicalRegion] {
        &self.regions[..self.region_count]
    }

    pub fn range_is_within_region_kind(&self, start: u64, end: u64, kind: RegionKind) -> bool {
        start < end
            && self
                .regions()
                .iter()
                .any(|region| region.start <= start && end <= region.end && region.kind == kind)
    }

    /// Check that a non-empty physical span is covered only by ACPI reclaimable
    /// or ACPI NVS regions, optionally enforcing the reported physical width.
    /// Adjacent ACPI regions may jointly cover the span; gaps and other region
    /// kinds are rejected. This checks metadata only and does not authorize a
    /// dereference or imply that the span is mapped.
    pub fn validate_acpi_span(
        &self,
        start: u64,
        length: u64,
        physical_address_bits: Option<u8>,
    ) -> Result<ValidatedAcpiSpan, AcpiSpanError> {
        const PAGE_SIZE: u64 = 4096;
        const MAX_SPAN_SIZE: u64 = 64 * 1024;

        if length == 0 {
            return Err(AcpiSpanError::EmptyRange);
        }
        if length > MAX_SPAN_SIZE {
            return Err(AcpiSpanError::SpanTooLarge);
        }
        let end = start
            .checked_add(length)
            .ok_or(AcpiSpanError::AddressOverflow)?;
        if physical_address_bits
            .is_some_and(|bits| !physical_range_fits_address_width(start, end, bits))
        {
            return Err(AcpiSpanError::PhysicalRangeOutOfBounds);
        }
        let mapping_start = start & !(PAGE_SIZE - 1);
        let mapping_end = end
            .checked_add(PAGE_SIZE - 1)
            .ok_or(AcpiSpanError::AddressOverflow)?
            & !(PAGE_SIZE - 1);
        if mapping_end <= mapping_start {
            return Err(AcpiSpanError::AddressOverflow);
        }
        if physical_address_bits.is_some_and(|bits| {
            !physical_range_fits_address_width(mapping_start, mapping_end, bits)
        }) {
            return Err(AcpiSpanError::PhysicalRangeOutOfBounds);
        }
        if !self.range_is_within_acpi_regions(mapping_start, mapping_end, physical_address_bits) {
            return Err(AcpiSpanError::NotFullyAcpiBacked);
        }

        Ok(ValidatedAcpiSpan {
            byte_start: start,
            byte_end: end,
            mapping_start,
            mapping_end,
            authorization: AcpiSpanAuthorization::AcpiTableMemory,
        })
    }

    /// Validate an RSDP span in ACPI memory or in the bounded legacy x86
    /// firmware window `[0x80000, 0x100000)`. The legacy exception is only for

    pub fn validate_rsdp_span(

            byte_start: start,
            byte_end: end,
            mapping_start,
            mapping_end,
            authorization: AcpiSpanAuthorization::LegacyRsdpReserved,
        })
    }

    /// Map one validated extent backed by an allocator-owned frame token.
    pub fn map_owned_page(
        &mut self,
        allocator: &mut FrameAllocator,
        frame: Frame,
        mapping: ValidatedMappingRange,
        cache_attributes: SelectedCacheAttributes,
    ) -> Result<(), CandidateMapFailure<M::Error>> {
        let backing = CandidateMappingBacking::Owned(frame);
        match self.map_page_with_backing(allocator, mapping, cache_attributes, backing, false) {
            Ok(()) => Ok(()),
            Err(failure) => match failure.backing {
                CandidateMappingBacking::Owned(frame) => Err(CandidateMapFailure {
                    cause: failure.cause,
                    frame,
                    rollback_retained_table_frames: failure.rollback_retained_table_frames,
                }),
                CandidateMappingBacking::BorrowedAcpi { .. }
                | CandidateMappingBacking::BorrowedMmio { .. } => {
                    unreachable!("owned map failure retains its owned frame token")
                }
            },
        }
    }

    /// Add an allocator-backed leaf to this active root and invalidate its local TLB entry.
    ///
    /// # Safety
    /// This candidate must be the current CR3 root on the current CPU. The caller
    /// must keep maskable interrupts disabled and serialize all page-table users
    /// until the operation returns. The memory backend must implement local TLB
    /// invalidation, and no other CPU may use this root.
    pub unsafe fn map_owned_page_active(
        &mut self,
        allocator: &mut FrameAllocator,
        frame: Frame,
        mapping: ValidatedMappingRange,
        cache_attributes: SelectedCacheAttributes,
    ) -> Result<(), CandidateMapFailure<M::Error>> {
        let backing = CandidateMappingBacking::Owned(frame);
        match self.map_page_with_backing(allocator, mapping, cache_attributes, backing, true) {
            Ok(()) => Ok(()),
            Err(failure) => match failure.backing {
                CandidateMappingBacking::Owned(frame) => Err(CandidateMapFailure {
                    cause: failure.cause,
                    frame,
                    rollback_retained_table_frames: failure.rollback_retained_table_frames,
                }),
                CandidateMappingBacking::BorrowedAcpi { .. }
                | CandidateMappingBacking::BorrowedMmio { .. } => {
                    unreachable!("owned active-map failure retains its frame token")
                }
            },
        }
    }

    /// Map one page from a validated ACPI extent without transferring ownership
    /// of the physical page to the allocator. The mapping is supervisor,
    /// read-only, and NX; it may only be installed while this candidate is inactive.
    pub fn map_acpi_page(
        &mut self,
        allocator: &mut FrameAllocator,
        span: ValidatedAcpiSpan,
        mapping: ValidatedMappingRange,
        cache_attributes: SelectedCacheAttributes,
    ) -> Result<(), CandidateBorrowedMapFailure<M::Error>> {
        if mapping.length() != PAGE_SIZE
            || !span.contains_mapping_page(mapping.physical_start)
            || mapping.physical_start.checked_add(PAGE_SIZE) != Some(mapping.physical_end)
            || mapping.physical_address_bits != self.config.physical_address_bits
            || mapping.permissions.writable
            || mapping.permissions.executable
            || mapping.permissions.user_accessible
        {
            return Err(CandidateBorrowedMapFailure {
                cause: CandidateMapErrorKind::InvalidMapping,
                rollback_retained_table_frames: 0,
            });
        }
        let backing = CandidateMappingBacking::BorrowedAcpi {
            physical_address: mapping.physical_start,
        };
        self.map_page_with_backing(allocator, mapping, cache_attributes, backing, false)
            .map_err(|failure| CandidateBorrowedMapFailure {
                cause: failure.cause,
                rollback_retained_table_frames: failure.rollback_retained_table_frames,
            })
    }

    /// Map one explicit device-register page without transferring frame ownership.
    /// The page is supervisor, writable, NX, and must use UC effective memory type.
    ///
    /// # Safety
    /// `mapping.physical_start` must be an assigned device-register page, not
    /// ordinary RAM or an unverified physical address. The caller must derive it
    /// from validated platform/device data and keep the cache policy consistent
    /// with every alias. The candidate must remain inactive until the mapping is
    /// installed; this method never makes an active mapping or invalidates TLBs.
    pub unsafe fn map_mmio_page(
        &mut self,

        if mapping.length() != PAGE_SIZE

            || mapping.physical_start.checked_add(PAGE_SIZE) != Some(mapping.physical_end)

            || !mapping.permissions.writable
            || mapping.permissions.executable
            || mapping.permissions.user_accessible

        {
            return Err(CandidateBorrowedMapFailure {
                cause: CandidateMapErrorKind::InvalidMapping,
                rollback_retained_table_frames: 0,
            });
        }
        let backing = CandidateMappingBacking::BorrowedMmio {
            physical_address: mapping.physical_start,
        };
        self.map_page_with_backing(allocator, mapping, cache_attributes, backing, false)
            .map_err(|failure| CandidateBorrowedMapFailure {
                cause: failure.cause,
                rollback_retained_table_frames: failure.rollback_retained_table_frames,
            })
    }

    fn map_page_with_backing(

            || mapping.physical_start != physical_address

```rust
/// region reported as usable RAM or as kernel/module/bootloader-reclaimable
/// memory, which Limine currently relies on. Device registers are expected to
/// lie in reserved/unknown address space (or to be absent from the map).
```

```rust
/// Validate that one aligned 4 KiB physical page is not reported as RAM or as
/// kernel/module/bootloader-reclaimable memory anywhere in the map.
pub fn validate_mmio_page(
    &self,
    physical_address: u64,
    physical_address_bits: Option<u8>,
) -> Result<ValidatedMmioSpan, MmioSpanError> {
    const PAGE_SIZE: u64 = 4096;
    if physical_address & (PAGE_SIZE - 1) != 0 {
        return Err(MmioSpanError::Misaligned);
    }
    let end = physical_address.checked_add(PAGE_SIZE).ok_or(MmioSpanError::AddressOverflow)?;
    if physical_address_bits.is_some_and(|bits| !physical_range_fits_address_width(physical_address, end, bits)) {
        return Err(MmioSpanError::PhysicalRangeOutOfBounds);
    }
    for region in self.regions() {
        if region.start < end && physical_address < region.end
            && matches!(region.kind, RegionKind::Usable | RegionKind::ExecutableAndModules | RegionKind::BootloaderReclaimable)
        {
            return Err(MmioSpanError::OverlapsRam);
        }
    }
    Ok(ValidatedMmioSpan { mapping_start: physical_address, mapping_end: end })
}
```

**sync.rs**: add INTERRUPT_DEPTH.

```rust
/// Number of exception/IRQ entry frames currently active on this CPU.
///
/// Maintained by the kernel binary's interrupt/exception entry paths. Used by
/// the heap to detect and reject (in debug builds) allocation attempts from
/// interrupt context, where taking the heap lock can deadlock the CPU. Not
/// SMP-safe; increments and decrements are not atomic with respect to each
/// other, but x86 aligned 8-byte loads/stores are atomic and the count only
/// matters on the entering/leaving CPU.
#[doc(hidden)]
pub static INTERRUPT_DEPTH: AtomicUsize = AtomicUsize::new(0);
```
Need `AtomicUsize` import — change `use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, Ordering};` to include AtomicUsize. And a guard helper:

```rust
/// RAII guard that records one active exception/IRQ frame in [`INTERRUPT_DEPTH`].
#[doc(hidden)]
pub struct InterruptContextGuard;
```
Hmm — but InterruptContextGuard with Drop is safe to use in extern "C"? rustix_irq_dispatch is `pub extern "C" fn` — creating a guard and letting it drop at function exit works (drop before returning). For exception dispatch, never returns — guard leaks the increment (depth stays 1) — that's intended.

Actually let me put the guard in sync.rs so heap.rs can see it? heap.rs only needs INTERRUPT_DEPTH read. Put both in sync.rs:

```rust
#[doc(hidden)]
pub struct InterruptContextGuard;

impl InterruptContextGuard {
    pub fn enter() -> Self {
        INTERRUPT_DEPTH.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
        Self
    }
}

impl Drop for InterruptContextGuard {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        INTERRUPT_DEPTH.fetch_sub(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
    }
}
```

77: /// A bounded allocator over only validated `Usable` memory-map ranges.
78: ///
79: /// For a given physical map, callers must use only one allocator instance as
80: /// the allocation authority; the type does not enforce this uniqueness. Within
81: /// that instance, each eligible frame is owned by the allocator while free, or
82: /// by the non-copyable `Frame` token returned by `allocate` while allocated. A
83: /// frame address is an identifier only; this allocator does not map or access it.
84: /// Both range tables are sorted, disjoint, page-aligned half-open ranges. The
85: /// allocator returns `None` on frame exhaustion and reports metadata exhaustion
86: /// explicitly without changing state or consuming the caller's frame token.
87: pub struct FrameAllocator {
88:     managed: [FrameRange; MAX_FRAME_RANGES],
89:     managed_count: usize,
90:     free: [FrameRange; MAX_FRAME_RANGES],
91:     free_count: usize,
92:     managed_frames: u64,
93:     free_frames: u64,
94: }
95: 
96: impl FrameAllocator {
97:     const EMPTY: Self = Self {
98:         managed: [FrameRange::EMPTY; MAX_FRAME_RANGES],
99:         managed_count: 0,
100:         free: [FrameRange::EMPTY; MAX_FRAME_RANGES],
101:         free_count: 0,
102:         managed_frames: 0,
103:         free_frames: 0,
104:     };
105: 
106:     /// Build allocator metadata without allocating or touching physical memory.
107:     ///
108:     /// The caller must ensure there is only one allocator instance per physical
109:     /// map; constructing multiple instances would duplicate allocation authority.
110:     pub fn from_memory_map(map: &MemoryMap) -> Result<Self, FrameError> {
111:         let mut allocator = Self::EMPTY;
112:         let page_mask = !(FRAME_SIZE - 1);
113: 
114:         for region in map.regions() {
115:             if region.kind() != RegionKind::Usable {
116:                 continue;
117:             }
118: 
119:             let start = region.start().max(LOW_MEMORY_RESERVED_END);
120:             let Some(start) = start
121:                 .checked_add(FRAME_SIZE - 1)
122:                 .map(|address| address & page_mask)
123:             else {
124:                 continue;
125:             };
126:             let end = region.end() & page_mask;
127:             if start >= end {
128:                 continue;
129:             }
130: 
131:             let range = FrameRange { start, end };
132:             Self::insert_range(&mut allocator.managed, &mut allocator.managed_count, range)?;
133:             Self::insert_range(&mut allocator.free, &mut allocator.free_count, range)?;
134: 
135:             let frames = (end - start) / FRAME_SIZE;
136:             allocator.managed_frames = allocator
137:                 .managed_frames
138:                 .checked_add(frames)
139:                 .ok_or(FrameError::AccountingOverflow)?;
140:             allocator.free_frames = allocator
141:                 .free_frames
142:                 .checked_add(frames)
143:                 .ok_or(FrameError::AccountingOverflow)?;
144:         }
145: 
146:         Ok(allocator)
147:     }
148: 
149:     /// Number of complete frames inside eligible usable regions.
150:     pub const fn managed_frames(&self) -> u64 {
151:         self.managed_frames
152:     }
153: 
154:     /// Number of currently unallocated frames.
155:     pub const fn free_frames(&self) -> u64 {
156:         self.free_frames
157:     }
158: 
159:     /// Allocate the lowest-address available frame, or return `None` on exhaustion.
160:     pub fn allocate(&mut self) -> Option<Frame> {
161:         if self.free_count == 0 {
162:             return None;
163:         }
164: 
165:         let address = self.free[0].start;
166:         self.free[0].start += FRAME_SIZE;
167:         debug_assert!(self.free_frames > 0);
168:         self.free_frames = self.free_frames.saturating_sub(1);
169:         if self.free[0].start == self.free[0].end {
170:             Self::remove_range(&mut self.free, &mut self.free_count, 0);
171:         }
172:         Some(Frame(address))
173:     }
174: 
175:     /// Return a frame to the allocator, merging adjacent free ranges.
176:     ///
177:     /// On error, the returned `DeallocationError` retains the frame token so the
178:     /// caller can retry or otherwise preserve ownership. Failed returns do not
179:     /// change allocator state.
180:     pub fn deallocate(&mut self, frame: Frame) -> Result<(), DeallocationError> {
181:         let address = frame.address();
182:         if address & (FRAME_SIZE - 1) != 0 {
183:             return Err(DeallocationError {
184:                 error: FrameError::UnalignedFrame,
185:                 frame,
186:             });
187:         }
188:         let Some(end) = address.checked_add(FRAME_SIZE) else {
189:             return Err(DeallocationError {
190:                 error: FrameError::NotManaged,
191:                 frame,
192:             });
193:         };
194:         if !self
195:             .managed
196:             .iter()
197:             .take(self.managed_count)
198:             .any(|range| range.start <= address && end <= range.end)
199:         {
200:             return Err(DeallocationError {
201:                 error: FrameError::NotManaged,
202:                 frame,
203:             });
204:         }
205:         if self
206:             .free
207:             .iter()
208:             .take(self.free_count)
209:             .any(|range| address < range.end && range.start < end)
210:         {
211:             return Err(DeallocationError {
212:                 error: FrameError::AlreadyFree,
213:                 frame,
214:             });
215:         }
216: 
217:         let Some(new_free_count) = self.free_frames.checked_add(1) else {
218:             return Err(DeallocationError {
219:                 error: FrameError::AccountingOverflow,
220:                 frame,
221:             });
222:         };
223:         if let Err(error) = Self::insert_range(
224:             &mut self.free,
225:             &mut self.free_count,
226:             FrameRange {
227:                 start: address,
228:                 end,
229:             },
230:         ) {
231:             return Err(DeallocationError { error, frame });
232:         }
233:         self.free_frames = new_free_count;
234:         Ok(())
235:     }
236: 
237:     fn insert_range(
238:         ranges: &mut [FrameRange; MAX_FRAME_RANGES],
239:         count: &mut usize,
240:         incoming: FrameRange,
241:     ) -> Result<(), FrameError> {
242:         let mut index = 0;
243:         while index < *count && ranges[index].start < incoming.start {
244:             index += 1;
245:         }
246: 
247:         if (index > 0 && ranges[index - 1].end > incoming.start)
248:             || (index < *count && incoming.end > ranges[index].start)
249:         {
250:             return Err(FrameError::RangeOverlap);
251:         }
252: 
253:         let joins_previous = index > 0 && ranges[index - 1].end == incoming.start;
254:         let joins_next = index < *count && incoming.end == ranges[index].start;
255:         match (joins_previous, joins_next) {
256:             (true, true) => {
257:                 ranges[index - 1].end = ranges[index].end;
258:                 Self::remove_range(ranges, count, index);
259:             }
260:             (true, false) => ranges[index - 1].end = incoming.end,
261:             (false, true) => ranges[index].start = incoming.start,
262:             (false, false) => {
263:                 if *count == MAX_FRAME_RANGES {
264:                     return Err(FrameError::RangeTableFull);
265:                 }
266:                 for move_index in (index..*count).rev() {
267:                     ranges[move_index + 1] = ranges[move_index];
268:                 }
269:                 ranges[index] = incoming;
270:                 *count += 1;
271:             }
272:         }
273:         Ok(())
274:     }
275: 
276:     fn remove_range(ranges: &mut [FrameRange; MAX_FRAME_RANGES], count: &mut usize, index: usize) {
277:         for move_index in index..(*count - 1) {
278:             ranges[move_index] = ranges[move_index + 1];
279:         }
280:         debug_assert!(*count > 0);
281:         *count -= 1;
282:         ranges[*count] = FrameRange::EMPTY;
283:     }
284: }

_Thinking:_

Now I'll implement `from_memory_map_excluding`. Design:

```rust
/// A half-open physical span that must never be handed out as a free frame.
///
/// Used to subtract loader-owned memory (kernel image, boot stack) that the
/// memory map may mislabel as `Usable`.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct MemoryExclusion {
    pub start: u64,
    pub end: u64,
}
```

Hmm — should exclusions be validated (start < end)? Yes, skip invalid ones or error? Error: define behavior — invalid (start >= end) exclusions are skipped (they exclude nothing)? No — a caller bug should be loud in debug, silent skip dangerous (thinking memory is reserved when it isn't). I'll make invalid exclusions an error? FrameError has no variant... add `InvalidExclusion` to FrameError enum. That's fine — enum is exhaustive in matches? Search for FrameError matches: NotManaged etc matched in tests and maybe main.rs? main.rs maps BootInfoError::FrameAllocator(FrameError) with Debug print. Adding a variant is fine (Debug derive, non-exhaustive use internally — any exhaustive match would fail to compile, which we'll catch).

Implementation in from_memory_map_excluding:

```rust
pub fn from_memory_map_excluding(
    map: &MemoryMap,
    exclusions: &[(u64, u64)],
) -> Result<Self, FrameError> {
    // Up to eight exclusions are supported: two per table row of the fixed
    // range tables cannot be accommodated, but subtraction only splits at
    // span boundaries, so the piece count stays bounded by exclusions + 1.
    ...
}
```

Subtraction algorithm per region:
```rust
const MAX_EXCLUSION_PIECES: usize = 8; // exclusions.len() <= 7
let mut pieces: [(u64, u64); 8];
pieces[0] = (start, end); let mut piece_count = 1;
for &(ex_start, ex_end) in exclusions {
    if ex_start >= ex_end { return Err(FrameError::InvalidExclusion); }
    let mut next = [(0u64, 0u64); 8]; let mut next_count = 0usize;
    for i in 0..piece_count {
        let (s, e) = pieces[i];
        // no overlap
        if ex_end <= s || ex_start >= e { next[next_count] = (s,e); next_count += 1; continue; }
        if s < ex_start { next[next_count] = (s, ex_start); next_count += 1; }
        if ex_end < e { next[next_count] = (ex_end, e); next_count += 1; }
    }

    piece_count = next_count;
```

```rust
/// Errors from validating a physical page span for MMIO mapping.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum MmioSpanError {
    Misaligned,
    AddressOverflow,
    PhysicalRangeOutOfBounds,
    /// The page is reported as ordinary usable RAM, a kernel/module image, or
    /// bootloader-reclaimable memory.
    OverlapsRam,
}

/// A physical page authorized for MMIO mapping: aligned 4 KiB, fits the known
/// physical-address width, and does not intersect any region reported as
/// `Usable`, `ExecutableAndModules`, or `BootloaderReclaimable`.
///
/// The kernel image is additionally excluded by callers at the mapping site
/// (it is not part of the memory map).
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ValidatedMmioSpan {
    mapping_start: u64,
    mapping_end: u64,
}

impl ValidatedMmioSpan {
    pub const fn mapping_start(self) -> u64 { self.mapping_start }
    pub const fn mapping_end(self) -> u64 { self.mapping_end }
    /// Whether one aligned 4 KiB physical page equals this validated span.
    pub fn contains_mapping_page(self, physical_page: u64) -> bool {
        physical_page == self.mapping_start
    }
}
```

In MemoryMap impl:

```rust
/// Validate one 4 KiB physical page as an MMIO page: it must be 4 KiB
/// aligned, fit the known physical-address width, and not intersect any
/// region classified as usable RAM or kernel/module/bootloader-reclaimable
/// memory. Device registers are expected to lie in reserved, unknown, or
/// unreported address space.
pub fn validate_mmio_page(
    &self,
    physical_address: u64,
    physical_address_bits: Option<u8>,
) -> Result<ValidatedMmioSpan, MmioSpanError> {
    const PAGE_SIZE: u64 = 4096;
    if physical_address & (PAGE_SIZE - 1) != 0 {
        return Err(MmioSpanError::Misaligned);
    }
    let mapping_end = physical_address
        .checked_add(PAGE_SIZE)
        .ok_or(MmioSpanError::AddressOverflow)?;
    if physical_address_bits.is_some_and(|bits| {
        !physical_range_fits_address_width(physical_address, mapping_end, bits)
    }) {
        return Err(MmioSpanError::PhysicalRangeOutOfBounds);
    }
    for region in self.regions() {
        if region.start < mapping_end && physical_address < region.end
            && matches!(
                region.kind,
                RegionKind::Usable
                    | RegionKind::ExecutableAndModules
                    | RegionKind::BootloaderReclaimable
            )
        {
            return Err(MmioSpanError::OverlapsRam);
        }
    }
    Ok(ValidatedMmioSpan {
        mapping_start: physical_address,
        mapping_end,
    })
}
```

Device registers are expected to lie in reserved, unknown, or\n    /// unreported address space.\n    pub fn validate_mmio_page(\n        &self,\n        physical_address: u64,\n        physical_address_bits: Option<u8>,\n    ) -> Result<ValidatedMmioSpan, MmioSpanError> {\n        const PAGE_SIZE: u64 = 4096;\n\n        if physical_address & (PAGE_SIZE - 1) != 0 {\n            return Err(MmioSpanError::Misaligned);\n        }\n        let mapping_end = physical_address\n            .checked_add(PAGE_SIZE)\n            .ok_or(MmioSpanError::AddressOverflow)?;\n        if physical_address_bits.is_some_and(|bits| {\n            !physical_range_fits_address_width(physical_address, mapping_end, bits)\n        }) {\n            return Err(MmioSpanError::PhysicalRangeOutOfBounds);\n        }\n        for region in self.regions() {\n            if region.start < mapping_end\n                && physical_address < region.end\n                && matches!(\n                    region.kind,\n                    RegionKind::Usable\n                        | RegionKind::ExecutableAndModules\n                        | RegionKind::BootloaderReclaimable\n                )\n            {\n                return Err(MmioSpanError::OverlapsRam);\n            }\n        }\n\n        Ok(ValidatedMmioSpan {\n            mapping_start: physical_address,\n            mapping_end,\n        })\n    }\n\n    /// Check that a non-empty physical span is covered only by ACPI reclaimable",

//! Checked x86_64 mapping policy and a bounded owned page-table builder.
//!
//! Pure helpers validate addresses, permissions, and cache policy. Candidate
//! table access is delegated to an unsafe backend contract. This module never
//! writes CR3; callers may activate a validated hierarchy through the explicit

```rust
pub extern "C" fn rustix_exception_dispatch(context: *const ExceptionContext) -> ! {

    ... page fault block (keep) ...
}
```

#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
struct FxSaveArea([u8; 512]);

impl FxSaveArea {
    const INITIAL: Self = {
        let mut bytes = [0; 512];
        bytes[0] = 0x7f;
        bytes[1] = 0x03;
        bytes[24] = 0x80;
        bytes[25] = 0x1f;

    old_rsp: *mut usize,

unsafe fn publish_fx_state(state: *mut FxSaveArea) {

    unsafe { ACTIVE_FX_STATE_POINTER = state };

unsafe fn save_bootstrap_fx_state() {
    let state = core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(BOOTSTRAP_FX_STATE);
    // SAFETY: FXSAVE is enabled during boot, `state` is 16-byte aligned, and the
    // caller has IF clear while the last kernel thread returns to the bootstrap.

    // SAFETY: Interrupts remain disabled until the saved bootstrap area is active.
    unsafe { publish_fx_state(state) };
}

//! x86_64 execution-context switching for cooperative and preempted threads.
//!
//! The switch preserves the SysV callee-saved general-purpose registers, stack
//! pointer, and x87/SSE state. It does not save flags, caller-saved general-
//! purpose registers, control registers, or address-space state. Timer
//! preemption uses a separate entry because the IRQ stub has already saved the
//! interrupted task's x87/SSE state before calling Rust.

core::arch::global_asm!(
    ".section .text.rustix_context_switch,\"ax\"",
    ".global rustix_context_switch",
    "rustix_context_switch:",
    "    push rbx",
    "    push rbp",
    "    push r12",
    "    push r13",
    "    push r14",
    "    push r15",
    "    fxsave64 [rdx]",
    "    mov [rdi], rsp",
    "    mov rsp, rsi",
    "    fxrstor64 [rcx]",
    "    pop r15",
    "    pop r14",
    "    pop r13",
    "    pop r12",
    "    pop rbp",
    "    pop rbx",
    "    cld",
    "    ret",
    ".global rustix_context_switch_from_irq",
    "rustix_context_switch_from_irq:",
    "    push rbx",
    "    push rbp",
    "    push r12",
    "    push r13",
    "    push r14",
    "    push r15",
    "    mov [rdi], rsp",
    "    mov rsp, rsi",
    "    fxrstor64 [rdx]",
    "    pop r15",
    "    pop r14",
    "    pop r13",
    "    pop r12",
    "    pop rbp",
    "    pop rbx",
    "    cld",
    "    ret",
);

extern "C" {
    /// Saves the current cooperative context and resumes another one.
    ///
    /// This is a normal SysV ABI function. `old_rsp` must point to a writable,
    /// pointer-aligned slot that remains valid for as long as the saved context
    /// may be resumed. `new_rsp` must point to a valid saved frame containing
    /// six register slots followed by a return address; a fresh frame can be
    /// prepared with `scheduler::initialize_thread_context_stack`. Both stacks must remain
    /// mapped, writable, and alive while their contexts can run. Fresh frames
    /// can be built with `scheduler::initialize_thread_context_stack`.
    ///
    /// # Safety
    ///
    /// Call only from ordinary thread context with local interrupts disabled.
    /// Both contexts must run on the same CPU, at the same privilege level, with
    /// compatible mappings and valid 16-byte-aligned FXSAVE areas. The trampoline
    /// used for a fresh context must not return. No stack or FXSAVE area may be
    /// freed while its context is saved or active.
    #[link_name = "rustix_context_switch"]
    pub fn context_switch(
        old_rsp: *mut usize,
        new_rsp: *mut usize,
        old_fx_state: *mut u8,
        new_fx_state: *const u8,
    );

    /// Switch from the configured timer IRQ path after its assembly stub saved
    /// the interrupted context's FXSAVE image. Does not save the handler's FP
    /// scratch state into the interrupted context.
    ///
    /// # Safety
    /// The same context and mapping requirements as [`context_switch`] apply.
    /// Call only from the timer IRQ with IF clear and pass an aligned initialized
    /// FXSAVE area for `new_fx_state`.
    #[link_name = "rustix_context_switch_from_irq"]
    pub fn context_switch_from_irq(
        old_rsp: *mut usize,
        new_rsp: *mut usize,
        new_fx_state: *const u8,
    );
}

    if reported_base_revision != 0 {
        return Err(BootInfoError::UnsupportedBaseRevision {
            reported: reported_base_revision,
            required: REQUIRED_BASE_REVISION,
        });
    }

    let cpu_info = crate::arch::x86_64::cpu::identify();
    let inherited_cr3_raw = crate::arch::x86_64::cpu::read_cr3_raw();
    let inherited_cr3_root = parse_cr3_root(
        inherited_cr3_raw,
        cpu_info.physical_address_bits,

    );
    let virtual_address_bits = cpu_info.active_linear_address_bits;
    let stack_size_request = core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(STACK_SIZE_REQUEST);
    // SAFETY: Limine completes supported request responses before `_start`.
    let stack_size_response_pointer =
        unsafe { core::ptr::read_volatile(core::ptr::addr_of!((*stack_size_request).response)) };
    if stack_size_response_pointer.is_null() {
        return Err(BootInfoError::MissingStackSizeResponse);
    }
    if !is_valid_pointer(stack_size_response_pointer, virtual_address_bits) {
        return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidStackSizeResponsePointer);
    }
    // SAFETY: The response pointer is non-null, aligned, and canonical. As with
    // the other Limine responses, readability relies on the handoff contract.
    let _stack_size_response = unsafe { stack_size_response_pointer.read_volatile() };
    let (boot_stack_virtual_start, boot_stack_virtual_end) =
        limine_x86_64_stack_bounds(entry_rsp, virtual_address_bits, REQUESTED_BOOT_STACK_SIZE)
            .ok_or(BootInfoError::InvalidInitialStackBounds)?;
    let hhdm_request = core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(HHDM_REQUEST);
    // SAFETY: Limine has completed writing the request response before `_start`.
    let hhdm_pointer =
        unsafe { core::ptr::read_volatile(core::ptr::addr_of!((*hhdm_request).response)) };
    if hhdm_pointer.is_null() {
        return Err(BootInfoError::MissingHhdmResponse);
    }
    if !is_valid_pointer(hhdm_pointer, virtual_address_bits) {
        return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidHhdmResponsePointer);
    }
        unsafe { core::ptr::read_volatile(core::ptr::addr_of!((*memory_map_request).response)) };
    if memory_map_pointer.is_null() {
        return Err(BootInfoError::MissingMemoryMapResponse);
    }
    if !is_valid_pointer(memory_map_pointer, virtual_address_bits) {
        return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidMemoryMapResponsePointer);
    }
    // SAFETY: The Limine response pointer is trusted to refer to a readable
    // response object; its nullness and alignment are checked above.
    let response = unsafe { memory_map_pointer.read_volatile() };
    let entry_count = usize::try_from(response.entry_count)
        .map_err(|_| BootInfoError::MemoryMapCountDoesNotFit)?;
    if entry_count > MAX_MEMORY_REGIONS {
        return Err(BootInfoError::TooManyMemoryRegions);
    }
    if entry_count == 0 {
        return Err(BootInfoError::EmptyMemoryMap);
    }
    if response.entries.is_null() {
        return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidEntriesPointer);
    }
    if !is_valid_pointer(response.entries, virtual_address_bits) {
        return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidEntriesPointer);
    }
    let entry_bytes = entry_count
        .checked_mul(size_of::<*const MemoryMapEntry>())
        .ok_or(BootInfoError::EntriesAddressOverflow)?;
    let entries_start = response.entries as usize;
    let entries_end = entries_start
        .checked_add(entry_bytes)
        .ok_or(BootInfoError::EntriesAddressOverflow)?;
    if !is_canonical_virtual_address(entries_start as u64, virtual_address_bits)
        || !is_canonical_virtual_address((entries_end - 1) as u64, virtual_address_bits)
    {
        return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidEntriesPointer);
    }

    let mut builder = MemoryMapBuilder::new();
    for index in 0..entry_count {
        // SAFETY: Limine promises an array of `entry_count` pointers. The count
        // is capped and pointer alignment/address arithmetic are checked above.
        let entry_pointer = unsafe { response.entries.add(index).read_volatile() };
        if !is_valid_pointer(entry_pointer, virtual_address_bits) {
            return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidEntryPointer { index });
        }
        // SAFETY: Limine promises each non-null entry pointer is readable.
        let entry = unsafe { entry_pointer.read_volatile() };
        builder
            .push(entry.base, entry.length, entry.kind)
            .map_err(BootInfoError::InvalidMemoryMap)?;
    }

    let memory_map = builder.finish().map_err(|error| match error {
        MemoryMapError::EmptyMap => BootInfoError::EmptyMemoryMap,
        other => BootInfoError::InvalidMemoryMap(other),
    })?;
    for (index, region) in memory_map.regions().iter().enumerate() {
        if let Some(physical_bits) = cpu_info.physical_address_bits {
            if !physical_range_fits_address_width(region.start(), region.end(), physical_bits) {
                return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidPhysicalRange { index });
            }
        }
        if region.kind().is_mapped_by_limine_v3_hhdm()
            && !limine_v3_hhdm_range_is_valid(
                hhdm.offset,
                region.start(),
                region.end(),
                cpu_info.physical_address_bits,
                virtual_address_bits,
            )
        {
            return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidHhdmMappingRange { index });
        }
    }
    let boot_stack_physical_start = boot_stack_virtual_start
        .checked_sub(hhdm.offset)
        .ok_or(BootInfoError::InvalidInitialStackHhdmRange)?;
    let boot_stack_physical_end = boot_stack_virtual_end
        .checked_sub(hhdm.offset)
        .ok_or(BootInfoError::InvalidInitialStackHhdmRange)?;
    if !limine_v3_hhdm_range_is_valid(
        hhdm.offset,
        boot_stack_physical_start,
        boot_stack_physical_end,
        cpu_info.physical_address_bits,
        virtual_address_bits,
    ) {
        return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidInitialStackHhdmRange);
    }
    if !memory_map.range_is_within_region_kind(
        boot_stack_physical_start,
        boot_stack_physical_end,
        RegionKind::BootloaderReclaimable,
    ) {
        return Err(BootInfoError::InitialStackNotBootloaderReclaimable);
    }
    let rsdp_request = core::ptr::addr_of_mut!(RSDP_REQUEST);
    // SAFETY: Limine completes supported request responses before `_start`.
    let rsdp_response_pointer =
        unsafe { core::ptr::read_volatile(core::ptr::addr_of!((*rsdp_request).response)) };
    let rsdp_physical_address = if rsdp_response_pointer.is_null() {
        None
    } else {
        if !is_valid_pointer(rsdp_response_pointer, virtual_address_bits) {
            return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidRsdpResponsePointer);
        }
        // SAFETY: Limine's response pointer is non-null, aligned, and canonical.
        // As for the other boot responses, readability relies on the handoff contract.
        let response = unsafe { rsdp_response_pointer.read_volatile() };
        if response.address.is_null() {
            return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidRsdpAddress);
        }
        let address = response.address as u64;
        if let Err(error) =
            memory_map.validate_rsdp_span(address, 20, cpu_info.physical_address_bits)
        {
            crate::kprintln!("RSDP physical span {:#x} rejected: {:?}", address, error);
            let page_start = address & !0xfff;
            let page_end = page_start.checked_add(4096);
            if let Some(page_end) = page_end {
                for region in memory_map.regions() {
                    if region.start() < page_end && page_start < region.end() {
                        crate::kprintln!(
                            "RSDP page intersects {:#x}..{:#x} ({:?})",
                            region.start(),
                            region.end(),
                            region.kind()
                        );
                    }
                }
            }
            return Err(BootInfoError::InvalidRsdpMappingSpan);
        }
        Some(address)
    };

    let frame_allocator =
        FrameAllocator::from_memory_map(&memory_map).map_err(BootInfoError::FrameAllocator)?;

    Ok(BootInfo {
        hhdm_revision: hhdm.revision,
        hhdm_offset: hhdm.offset,
        inherited_cr3_raw,
        inherited_cr3_root,
        executable_address_revision: executable_address.revision,
        kernel_physical_base: executable_address.physical_base,
        kernel_physical_end,
        kernel_virtual_base: executable_address.virtual_base,
        kernel_virtual_end: linked_virtual_end,
        kernel_image_segments,
        boot_stack_physical_start,
        boot_stack_physical_end,
        boot_stack_virtual_start,
        boot_stack_virtual_end,
        memory_map_revision: response.revision,
        rsdp_physical_address,
        acpi_madt: None,
        memory_map,
        frame_allocator,
        cpu_info,
    })
}

/// Print a report from the copied snapshot; no loader pointers are used here.
pub fn report_boot_information(boot_info: &BootInfo) {
    crate::kprintln!(
        "HHDM: offset {:#018x}, response revision {}",
        boot_info.hhdm_offset,
        boot_info.hhdm_revision
    );
    crate::kprintln!(
        "inherited CR3: raw {:#018x} (not walked during boot snapshot)",
        boot_info.inherited_cr3_raw
    );
    match boot_info.inherited_cr3_root {
        Ok(root) => crate::kprintln!(
            "inherited CR3 root: physical {:#018x}, low bits {:?}",
            root.physical_address(),
            root.low_bits()
        ),
        Err(error) => crate::kprintln!("inherited CR3 root could not be decoded: {:?}", error),
    }
    crate::kprintln!(
        "kernel image: physical [{:#018x}, {:#018x}), virtual [{:#018x}, {:#018x}), response revision {}",
        boot_info.kernel_physical_base,
        boot_info.kernel_physical_end,
        boot_info.kernel_virtual_base,
        boot_info.kernel_virtual_end,
        boot_info.executable_address_revision
    );
    let segments = &boot_info.kernel_image_segments;

fn map_bootstrap_apic_pages<'memory, 'map>(
    candidate: &mut rustix_kernel::paging::CandidatePageTables<
        'memory,
        LimineHhdmCandidateMemory<'map>,
    >,
    allocator: &mut rustix_kernel::frame::FrameAllocator,
    cpu_info: &arch::x86_64::cpu::CpuInfo,
    madt: &rustix_kernel::acpi::Madt,
) -> Result<(u64, u64), &'static str> {
    use rustix_kernel::paging::{
        validate_mapping_range, CachePolicy, EffectiveMemoryType, MtrrState, PagePermissions,
        PageWalkOutcome, PatLayout, PAGE_SIZE,
    };

    let physical_address_bits = cpu_info
        .physical_address_bits
        .ok_or("unknown physical width")?;
    let pat_value = cpu_info.pat_msr_value.ok_or("PAT unavailable")?;
    let pat = PatLayout::from_msr_value(pat_value).map_err(|_| "invalid PAT state")?;
    let cache_layout = match &cpu_info.mtrr {
        MtrrState::Captured(registers) => registers
            .validated_layout(physical_address_bits)
            .map_err(|_| "invalid MTRR state")?,
        _ => return Err("MTRR state unavailable"),
    };
    let mut io_apics = madt.io_apics();
    let io_apic = io_apics.next().ok_or("MADT has no I/O APIC")?;
    if io_apics.next().is_some() {
        return Err("multiple I/O APICs are unsupported by bootstrap routing");
    }

    let pages = [
        (LOCAL_APIC_VIRTUAL_ADDRESS, madt.local_apic_address),
fn rollback_kernel_heap_pages(
    candidate: &mut rustix_kernel::paging::CandidatePageTables<
        'static,
        LimineHhdmCandidateMemory<'static>,
    >,
    allocator: &mut rustix_kernel::frame::FrameAllocator,
    first_page: usize,
    mapped_pages: usize,
) {
    use rustix_kernel::paging::PAGE_SIZE;

    for page in (first_page..first_page + mapped_pages).rev() {
        let virtual_address = KERNEL_HEAP_GROWTH_VIRTUAL_BASE + page as u64 * PAGE_SIZE;
        // SAFETY: The pages belong to this transaction in the current active
        // root; unmapping invalidates their TLB entries before token return.
        let frame = unsafe { candidate.unmap_owned_page_active(virtual_address) }
            .unwrap_or_else(|_| halt_forever());
        deallocate_kernel_heap_frame(allocator, frame);
    }
}

fn deallocate_kernel_heap_frame(
    allocator: &mut rustix_kernel::frame::FrameAllocator,
    frame: rustix_kernel::frame::Frame,
) {
    if allocator.deallocate(frame).is_err() {
        halt_forever();
    }
}

```rust
    fn panic(info: &PanicInfo<'_>) -> ! {
        // Mask maskable interrupts before running any diagnostics: the kernel's
        // fatal paths must not be preempted mid-report, and the machine must
        // not resume other work after a panic is declared.
        unsafe { core::arch::asm!("cli", options(nomem, nostack)) };
        // A panic raised from the fatal path itself stops silently.
        if PANIC_HANDLER_ACTIVE.swap(true, Ordering::Acquire) {
            loop {
                unsafe { core::arch::asm!("cli; hlt", options(nomem, nostack)) };
            }
        }
        arch::x86_64::serial::initialize();
        kprintln!("KERNEL PANIC: {}", info);
        halt_forever()
    }
```
static PANIC_HANDLER_ACTIVE near panic fn — but statics at module level; put right before #[panic_handler].

```json
                }
```

            if let Err(failure) =

                rollback_kernel_heap_pages(candidate, allocator, first_page, mapped);
                return None;
            }
            mapped += 1;

        Some(())

3. print_file_contents sanitize:
```rust
fn print_file_contents(text: &str) {
    for (index, line) in text.split('\n').enumerate() {
        if index != 0 {
            serial::print(format_args!("\r\n"));
        }
        let line = line.strip_suffix('\r').unwrap_or(line);
        for character in line.chars() {
            if character.is_control() && character != '\t' {
                serial::print(format_args!("."));
            } else {
                serial::print(format_args!("{}", character));
            }
        }
    }
    if !text.ends_with('\n') {
        crate::kprintln!();
    }
}
```

fn two_paths(rest: &str) -> Result<(&str, &str), ParseError> {
    let (source, rest) = take_word(rest).ok_or(ParseError::MissingArgument)?;
    let (destination, rest) = take_word(rest).ok_or(ParseError::MissingArgument)?;
    no_arguments(rest)?;
    Ok((source, destination))
}

fn path_and_text(rest: &str) -> Result<(&str, &str), ParseError> {
    let (path, text) = take_word(rest).ok_or(ParseError::MissingArgument)?;
    if text.is_empty() {
        return Err(ParseError::MissingText);
    }

    let first = text.as_bytes()[0];
    if first == b'\'' || first == b'"' {
        if text.len() < 2 || text.as_bytes()[text.len() - 1] != first {
            return Err(ParseError::UnterminatedQuote);
        }
        return Ok((path, &text[1..text.len() - 1]));
    }

    Ok((path, text))
}

/// must be the validated table used by `initialize`, the source must remain
/// disabled until its handler is ready, and no other code may reprogram the APIC.
pub unsafe fn configure_isa_irq(madt: &Madt, isa_irq: u8, vector: u8) -> Result<(), ApicError> {
    if !interrupts_are_disabled() {
        return Err(ApicError::InterruptsEnabled);
    }
    if !INITIALIZED.load(Ordering::Acquire) {
        return Err(ApicError::NotInitialized);
    }
    if isa_irq >= 16 {
        return Err(ApicError::InvalidIsaIrq);
    }
    if vector < 32 || vector == APIC_SPURIOUS_VECTOR {
        return Err(ApicError::InvalidVector);
    }

    let route = madt.resolve_isa_interrupt(isa_irq);
    let gsi_base = IO_APIC_GSI_BASE.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
    let max_index = IO_APIC_MAX_REDIRECTION.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
    let last_gsi = gsi_base
        .checked_add(u32::from(max_index))
        .ok_or(ApicError::GsiRangeOverflow)?;
    if route.global_system_interrupt < gsi_base || route.global_system_interrupt > last_gsi {
        return Err(ApicError::IsaRouteOutOfRange);
    }
    let pin = (route.global_system_interrupt - gsi_base) as u8;
    let (low_register, high_register) =
        redirection_registers(pin, max_index).ok_or(ApicError::IsaRouteOutOfRange)?;
    let polarity = route.polarity;
    let trigger = route.trigger;
    let low = encode_redirection(vector, polarity, trigger, true);
    let destination = BOOTSTRAP_APIC_ID.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
    let io_apic_virtual = IO_APIC_VIRTUAL.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
    // SAFETY: Initialization validated the single I/O APIC mapping and masked
    // every entry. The caller keeps this source disabled until its handler is ready.
    unsafe {
        io_apic_write(io_apic_virtual, high_register, u32::from(destination) << 24);
        io_apic_write(io_apic_virtual, low_register, low);
    }
    ISA_ROUTE_LOW_REGISTERS[usize::from(isa_irq)].store(low_register, Ordering::Release);
    Ok(())
}

/// Unmask a previously configured MADT-resolved ISA IRQ route.
///
/// # Safety
/// Must run on the bootstrap CPU with IF=0 after the matching handler and device
/// state are ready. The APIC mapping and route must remain exclusively owned.
pub unsafe fn unmask_isa_irq(isa_irq: u8, vector: u8) -> Result<(), ApicError> {
    if !interrupts_are_disabled() {
        return Err(ApicError::InterruptsEnabled);
    }
    if !INITIALIZED.load(Ordering::Acquire) {
        return Err(ApicError::NotInitialized);
    }
    if isa_irq >= 16 {
        return Err(ApicError::InvalidIsaIrq);
    }
    if vector < 32 || vector == APIC_SPURIOUS_VECTOR {
        return Err(ApicError::InvalidVector);
    }
    let low_register = ISA_ROUTE_LOW_REGISTERS[usize::from(isa_irq)].load(Ordering::Acquire);
    if low_register == 0 {
        return Err(ApicError::IsaRouteNotConfigured);
    }
    let io_apic_virtual = IO_APIC_VIRTUAL.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
    let entry = unsafe { io_apic_read(io_apic_virtual, low_register) };
    if entry as u8 != vector || entry & IO_APIC_REDIR_MASKED == 0 {
        return Err(ApicError::IsaRouteNotConfigured);
    }
    unsafe { io_apic_write(io_apic_virtual, low_register, entry & !IO_APIC_REDIR_MASKED) };
    Ok(())
}
