/// # Safety
/// Must execute at CPL 0 on the bootstrap processor with maskable interrupts
/// disabled, after successful [`configure_pit_timer`]. The I/O APIC mapping
/// must remain valid and no other code may modify its redirection table.
pub unsafe fn unmask_pit_timer() -> Result<(), ApicError> {
    if !interrupts_are_disabled() {
        return Err(ApicError::InterruptsEnabled);
    }
    if !INITIALIZED.load(Ordering::Acquire) {
        return Err(ApicError::NotInitialized);
    }
    if !TIMER_CONFIGURED.load(Ordering::Acquire) {
        return Err(ApicError::TimerNotConfigured);
    }

    let io_apic_virtual = IO_APIC_VIRTUAL.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
    let low_register = TIMER_LOW_REGISTER.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
    let entry = unsafe { io_apic_read(io_apic_virtual, low_register) };
    unsafe { io_apic_write(io_apic_virtual, low_register, entry & !IO_APIC_REDIR_MASKED) };
    Ok(())
}

/// Mask the configured ISA IRQ0 route after a timer startup failure.
///
/// # Safety
/// Must execute at CPL 0 on the bootstrap processor with maskable interrupts
/// disabled after successful [`initialize`]. The I/O APIC mapping must remain
/// valid and no other code may modify its redirection table.
pub unsafe fn mask_pit_timer() -> Result<(), ApicError> {
    if !interrupts_are_disabled() {
        return Err(ApicError::InterruptsEnabled);
    }
    if !INITIALIZED.load(Ordering::Acquire) {
        return Err(ApicError::NotInitialized);
    }

    let io_apic_virtual = IO_APIC_VIRTUAL.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
    let low_register = TIMER_LOW_REGISTER.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
    let entry = unsafe { io_apic_read(io_apic_virtual, low_register) };
    unsafe { io_apic_write(io_apic_virtual, low_register, entry | IO_APIC_REDIR_MASKED) };
    TIMER_CONFIGURED.store(false, Ordering::Release);
    Ok(())
}

unsafe fn mask_all_io_apic_entries(base: u64, max_redirection_index: u8) -> Result<(), ApicError> {
    let mut pin = 0;
    while pin <= max_redirection_index {
        let (low, _) = redirection_registers(pin, max_redirection_index)

        pin += 1;
    }

}

unsafe fn mask_local_apic_lvt_entries(base: u64) {
    for offset in [
        APIC_LVT_TIMER,
        APIC_LVT_THERMAL,
        APIC_LVT_PERFORMANCE,
        APIC_LVT_LINT0,
        APIC_LVT_LINT1,
        APIC_LVT_ERROR,
    ] {
        let value = unsafe { read_local(base, offset) };
        unsafe { write_local(base, offset, u64::from(value | APIC_LVT_MASKED)) };
    }
}

fn redirection_registers(pin: u8, max_redirection_index: u8) -> Option<(u8, u8)> {

pub unsafe fn configure_pit_timer(vector: u8, divisor: u16) -> Result<(), ApicError> {
    if !interrupts_are_disabled() {
        return Err(ApicError::InterruptsEnabled);
    }
    if !INITIALIZED.load(Ordering::Acquire) {
        return Err(ApicError::NotInitialized);
    }
    if vector < 32 || vector == APIC_SPURIOUS_VECTOR {
        return Err(ApicError::InvalidVector);
    }

    let io_apic_virtual = IO_APIC_VIRTUAL.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
    let low_register = TIMER_LOW_REGISTER.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
    let high_register = low_register
        .checked_add(1)
        .ok_or(ApicError::TimerGsiOutOfRange)?;
    let destination = BOOTSTRAP_APIC_ID.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
    let attributes = TIMER_ROUTE_ATTRIBUTES.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
    let polarity = if attributes & 1 != 0 {
        InterruptPolarity::ActiveLow
    } else {
        InterruptPolarity::ActiveHigh
    };
    let trigger = if attributes & 2 != 0 {
        InterruptTrigger::Level
    } else {
        InterruptTrigger::Edge
    };
    let route_low = encode_redirection(vector, polarity, trigger, true);

    // Install the destination first, then the masked low dword so no transient
    // unmasked route can be observed.
    unsafe {
        io_apic_write(io_apic_virtual, high_register, u32::from(destination) << 24);
        io_apic_write(io_apic_virtual, low_register, route_low);
        io::write_u8(PIT_COMMAND, PIT_RATE_GENERATOR_LOHI);
        io::write_u8(PIT_CHANNEL_0, divisor as u8);
        io::write_u8(PIT_CHANNEL_0, (divisor >> 8) as u8);
    }

    TIMER_CONFIGURED.store(true, Ordering::Release);
    Ok(())
}

/// Unmask only the IRQ0 redirection entry validated by [`initialize`].
const IO_APIC_MAX_REDIRECTIONS: u8 = 120;

const PIC1_DATA: u16 = 0x21;
const PIC2_DATA: u16 = 0xa1;
const PIT_CHANNEL_0: u16 = 0x40;
const PIT_COMMAND: u16 = 0x43;
const PIT_RATE_GENERATOR_LOHI: u8 = 0x34;

static INITIALIZED: AtomicBool = AtomicBool::new(false);
static TIMER_CONFIGURED: AtomicBool = AtomicBool::new(false);
static LOCAL_APIC_VIRTUAL: AtomicU64 = AtomicU64::new(0);
static IO_APIC_VIRTUAL: AtomicU64 = AtomicU64::new(0);
static TIMER_LOW_REGISTER: AtomicU8 = AtomicU8::new(0);
static TIMER_ROUTE_ATTRIBUTES: AtomicU8 = AtomicU8::new(0);
static BOOTSTRAP_APIC_ID: AtomicU8 = AtomicU8::new(0);
static IO_APIC_GSI_BASE: AtomicU32 = AtomicU32::new(0);
static IO_APIC_MAX_REDIRECTION: AtomicU8 = AtomicU8::new(0);
static ISA_ROUTE_LOW_REGISTERS: [AtomicU8; 16] = [const { AtomicU8::new(0) }; 16];

/// Errors reported while validating or configuring the bootstrap APIC route.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
    io_apic_virtual: u64,
    bootstrap_apic_id: u8,
) -> Result<(), ApicError> {
    if !interrupts_are_disabled() {
        return Err(ApicError::InterruptsEnabled);
    }
    if INITIALIZED.load(Ordering::Acquire) {
        return Err(ApicError::AlreadyInitialized);
    }

    TIMER_CONFIGURED.store(false, Ordering::Relaxed);
    IO_APIC_GSI_BASE.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
    IO_APIC_MAX_REDIRECTION.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
    for route in &ISA_ROUTE_LOW_REGISTERS {
        route.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
    }
    LOCAL_APIC_VIRTUAL.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
    IO_APIC_VIRTUAL.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
    TIMER_LOW_REGISTER.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);

    // Fail closed for legacy interrupt delivery before inspecting the APICs.
    // SAFETY: The caller executes at CPL 0 and the standard PIC data ports are
    // valid; writing 0xff masks every 8259 input.
    unsafe {
        io::write_u8(PIC1_DATA, 0xff);
        io::write_u8(PIC2_DATA, 0xff);

        assert!(MemoryMap::from_raw(&adjacent).is_ok());

        let mut builder = MemoryMapBuilder::new();
        for index in 0..MAX_MEMORY_REGIONS {
            builder.push((index as u64) * 0x2000, 0x1000, 1).unwrap();
        }
        assert_eq!(
            builder.push(0x1_0000_0000, 0x1000, 1),
            Err(MemoryMapError::TooManyRegions)
        );
    }
}

## Assistant

**Tool: shell**

**Input:**
```json
{
  "command": "sed -n 30,75p docs/architecture/crabshell.md"
}
```

| `help` | List available commands and limits. |
| `clear` | Send the ANSI clear-screen/home sequence. |
| `pwd` | Print the absolute working directory. |
| `ls [PATH]` | List a directory; a file path prints that file's name and size. Defaults to the current directory. |
| `cd PATH` | Change to an existing directory. Supports absolute and relative paths, `.`, and `..`. |
| `mkdir PATH` | Create one directory; its parent must already exist. |
| `touch PATH` | Create an empty file, or leave an existing file unchanged. |
| `write PATH TEXT` | Create a file if needed and replace its contents with the remaining line. Matching outer single or double quotes around the text are removed. |
| `append PATH TEXT` | Create a file if needed and append the remaining line. |
| `cp SOURCE DEST` | Copy a file; an existing destination file is replaced. |
| `mv SOURCE DEST` | Move/rename a file; directories and existing destinations are rejected. |
| `cat PATH` | Print a UTF-8 text file. Binary data is not displayed. |
| `rm PATH` | Remove a file. Directories are rejected. |
| `rmdir PATH` | Remove an empty directory other than the current directory. |

Example:

```text
root@rustix:/home/rustix# mkdir notes
root@rustix:/home/rustix# write notes/hello.txt "hello from Rustix"
root@rustix:/home/rustix# cat notes/hello.txt
hello from Rustix
root@rustix:/home/rustix# cd ..
root@rustix:/home# pwd
/home
```

The grammar intentionally does not yet support quoted paths, escaped quotes, variables, pipes, redirection, globbing, command substitution, history, or a process launcher. Input is a single ASCII line (up to 256 bytes) from COM1's bounded IRQ queue, with polling fallback; PS/2 IRQ bytes are reported as raw scancodes rather than translated into characters. A scancode diagnostic redraws the prompt and any partially typed line.

## RAM filesystem contract

- The root plus at most 31 live entries fit in the fixed 32-node table.
- Path components are limited to 31 UTF-8 bytes; file contents are limited to 1024 bytes each.
- Paths may be absolute or relative and support repeated `/`, `.`, and `..`.
- `write` replaces a file; `append` adds bytes; `touch` never truncates. Oversized path-based writes and appends fail before creating a missing file, and failed capacity checks leave existing contents unchanged.
- `cp` copies files and replaces an existing destination file. `mv` moves files only and refuses to overwrite an existing destination. `rm` handles files only. `rmdir` refuses non-empty directories and the current directory.
- Filesystem allocation is fixed-capacity; errors are reported rather than allocating a heap.
- Every file and directory disappears when the machine resets. There is no block device, journaling, metadata persistence, permission model, links, or concurrent access yet.

## Tests and limitations

Host-side tests cover path traversal, file create/write/append/copy/move/remove, entry kinds, path rendering, directory emptiness, capacity failures without namespace mutation, and the parser's syntax/arity. The automated QEMU smoke test checks that the serial shell reaches its prompt; `just input-smoke` additionally exercises UART/PS/2 IRQ wakeup and raw scancode reporting. A separate manual `just run` session exercised `help`, `ls`, `meminfo`, `mkdir`, `cd`, `touch`, `write`, `append`, `cat`, `cp`, `mv`, `rm`, `rmdir`, and `pwd`; `cat` returned the appended text, `cp` replaced an existing destination with source contents, `rmdir` refused a nonempty directory, and `pwd` returned to `/home/rustix` after cleanup. The unsupported `memtest` command produced the expected unknown-command response. These checks do not emulate reboot persistence, exhaustively test shell interactions, or prove isolation. Since the shell runs in ring 0, a parser or filesystem bug can panic the entire kernel. This implementation must not be presented as a secure or POSIX-compliant shell.

docs/architecture/memory.md:7:`src/frame.rs` provides a fixed-capacity allocator core with host-side tests. It only manages complete 4 KiB frames inside validated `Usable` regions, reserves the first MiB, and coalesces adjacent free ranges. The ownership contract assumes one allocator instance per physical map: within that instance, each eligible frame is owned either by its free-range table or by a non-copyable `Frame` token while allocated. The public constructor does not enforce unique instances, so callers must not initialize multiple allocators for the same physical map; the boot path retains one allocator in `BootInfo`. `allocate` returns `None` when no free frame remains. `deallocate` consumes the token only on success; on any error, including free-range-table exhaustion, it returns a `DeallocationError` containing the original token and leaves allocator state unchanged. The range tables each have a fixed `MAX_FRAME_RANGES` capacity, so fragmentation can prevent a frame from being returned until nearby ranges coalesce. The active cloned page-table hierarchy owns its page-table frames; the frame allocator is otherwise not yet a general-purpose virtual-memory service.
docs/architecture/memory.md:47:2. **Frame allocator integration:** the bounded core is initialized from `BootInfo`; table and leaf mappings use allocator-issued tokens. The active mapping smoke validates one owned page's map/access/unmap/reclamation path.
docs/architecture/memory.md:63:The active root is a full clone of Limine's reachable hierarchy with kernel HHDM alias W^X hardening, not a clean-room Rustix address space. Heap growth is bounded to a fixed virtual reservation and 1 MiB of frame-backed pages. Broader alias/MMIO coverage, frame reclamation, and physical-hardware validation remain outstanding.
docs/index.md:22:The repository contains a bare-metal `no_std` Rust kernel for `x86_64-unknown-none`. After Limine handoff it validates and copies memory-map metadata into an owned snapshot, captures the executable's physical and virtual bases, complete linker-defined image span, and page-aligned text/rodata/data segment bounds, reports baseline CPUID features, active/maximum address widths, read-only CR0 caching state, gated EFER.NXE/IA32_PAT values, and a bounded MTRR snapshot, seeds a volatile RAM filesystem, and runs Crabshell over the COM1 serial console. Boot-protocol pointers are checked for canonicality using the active 4- or 5-level paging width; physical map bounds and protocol-scoped HHDM range arithmetic are also validated. Crabshell's allocation-free parser is a separate crate; the kernel handles command execution. On 2026-10-16, `just verify` passed in WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04 both in the working tree and from a clean detached worktree with an empty `target` directory; the clean run downloaded and built the pinned Limine host utility, created the image, and passed the bounded QEMU boot to the shell prompt. The serial log showed memory-map and CPU reporting. GitHub Actions run [#5](https://github.com/JustEthanCodes/rustix/actions/runs/42288095710) passed `just verify` on the published `dev` commit on 2026-11-06; it does not cover later uncommitted changes. A separate interactive QEMU session exercised `help`, `ls`, `meminfo`, directory navigation, and file create/write/append/read/copy/move/remove commands. Milestone 0's local exit criteria are met, and Milestone 1's validation criteria are met for the current Limine path. This is a kernel-mode shell, not a user process. A bounded physical-frame allocator is initialized from the validated map and reported by `meminfo`; token ownership and failed-deallocation semantics are documented and host-tested. The boot transition owns an activated clone of Limine's reachable page-table hierarchy, and the global heap begins with a 256 KiB BSS arena and 128 allocation records, with bounded frame-backed growth up to 1 MiB. The Limine base-revision 3 HHDM scope and a conservative initial mapping/alias/cache policy are documented. Pure host-tested helpers validate mapping ranges/PTE bits, parse PAT layouts, validate captured MTRR snapshots, combine PAT/MTRR types under normal caching according to Intel SDM Table 11-7, and select a PAT index only when it yields one effective type across an aligned physical range. Boot validates the captured state and prints a diagnostic effective type at PA 0. The transition preserves inherited cache attributes, adjusts kernel-image HHDM aliases to R/NX or RW/NX, and checks the effective PAT index against the inherited mapping. `BootInfo` records raw CR3 and a validated decode of its root physical address and low-bit interpretation. Checked HHDM access is limited to complete allocator-owned usable frames and cloned table frames; the active root owns its cloned table pages while inherited leaf frames remain loader-owned. The complete linker-defined kernel-image span and requested 256 KiB initial boot-stack span are captured and validated; Limine does not report any larger allocation bounds or guard status, so Rustix does not infer a guard page. The heap has bounded frame-backed growth and an IRQ-save lock; allocation remains prohibited in IRQ/exception context. ACPI support includes bounded host-tested RSDP/SDT/RSDT/XSDT/MADT parsing and a read-only/NX boot reader that copies validated MADT data into owned state. Rustix has a tested single-vCPU APIC/PIT IRQ path, cooperative and timer-preemptive scheduling, IRQ-save locks, guarded kernel threads, bounded frame-backed heap growth, and interrupt-driven UART/PS/2 queues with a polling fallback. SMP, process isolation, a syscall ABI, persistent filesystem, and Unix compatibility remain absent. Fatal CPU exceptions have basic GDT/TSS/IDT diagnostics but no recovery. `just fault-smoke` validates a controlled page fault (including CR2) and invalid opcode in QEMU; broader exception coverage and physical-hardware validation remain pending. On 2026-10-21, `just verify` passed after adding CR3 decoding and the pure MTRR resolver, including host tests, target check, release build, ISO creation, and QEMU prompt smoke. On 2026-10-22, `just verify` passed after adding bounded read-only MTRR register capture and validated PAT/MTRR policy resolution. On 2026-10-24, the final `just verify`, `just frame-smoke`, and `just fault-smoke` runs passed after review. QEMU verified the 256 KiB stack request, cloned-root CR3 transition, kernel HHDM alias hardening, heap alignment/reuse/exhaustion probe, and shell prompt; the current emulator reports CR4.PGE clear, so it exercised CR3 invalidation with PGE disabled, while the PGE-toggle path was statically reviewed but not exercised. Milestone 2's local exit criteria are met. Hosted CI passed on the published `dev` commit, but it does not cover today's uncommitted work or establish physical-hardware behavior, a clean-room mapping policy, or exhaustive alias coverage. On 2026-11-07, `just verify` passed with 127 host tests and BIOS/OVMF QEMU coverage for APIC/PIT delivery, active mapping/TLB invalidation, cooperative scheduling, and UART/PS/2 input wakeups. On 2026-11-13, `just verify` passed with 130 host tests and extended OVMF coverage for IRQ-save lock IF restoration, non-yielding timer preemption, x87/SSE context preservation, and input wakeups. Milestone 3's single-vCPU exit criteria are met; SMP, process isolation, and physical-hardware validation remain pending.
docs/architecture/boot.md:14:4. Before relying on Limine base-revision-3 semantics, the kernel volatile-reads the loader-updated request field and requires its supported marker to be `0` (unsupported loaders leave the requested value nonzero). It then reads CR0 caching-control state, CPU address widths, active `CR4.PCIDE`, and raw CR3. It decodes CR3's physical root address and low-bit meaning against those CPU properties, without dereferencing the root. It reads EFER.NXE only when CPUID reports NX, `IA32_PAT` only when CPUID reports PAT, and MTRR registers only when CPUID reports MTRR support; these reads do not change CPU state. The MTRR snapshot is bounded to 32 variable pairs and reports an explicit capacity-exceeded state without retaining partial registers. It requires and validates the stack-size response, copies the HHDM, executable-address, and memory-map responses into an owned `BootInfo` snapshot, validates pointers against the active linear width, derives and validates the linker-defined kernel image span and its page-aligned text/rodata/data segment spans from Limine's executable bases, and derives the requested 256 KiB initial-stack span from the captured entry `RSP`. If the optional RSDP response is present, it treats its address as physical under validated base revision 3 and validates the initial 20-byte span and its complete page-rounded mapping extent against ACPI-reclaimable/NVS map regions and the known physical width. Before the permanent CR3 switch, a temporary reader maps validated ACPI pages read-only/NX in the candidate root, reads and validates RSDP → RSDT/XSDT → MADT, copies the parsed MADT into `BootInfo`, and removes each borrowed mapping after restoring the prior CR3. It validates physical bounds, stack HHDM arithmetic/coverage, and page-rounded HHDM arithmetic for protocol-mapped memory types. It then builds a frame allocator only from validated usable ranges and rejects malformed metadata before starting the shell.
docs/architecture/boot.md:41:The kernel snapshots and validates the Limine map and preserves firmware and loader-reclaimable classifications without reclaiming them. The frame allocator manages only validated `Usable` regions; cloned page-table frames are retained as owned tokens. Loader-reclaimable memory remains unavailable until Rustix has copied any needed boot data and explicitly reaches a documented reclaim point; ACPI reclaimable memory needs the ACPI tables to be copied or parsed first.

Avoid widening `unsafe impl` or introducing raw-pointer-based public APIs without a documented safety argument. External boot inputs, user pointers, MMIO, and DMA must not be treated as trusted merely because current boot data is trusted.\n\n## Audit hardening (2026-11-16)\n\nA full static security audit (boot trust boundary, allocators, paging, interrupts/scheduler, drivers, shell/VFS, build/CI) was performed and its actionable findings were applied. Highlights:\n\n- The panic handler masks maskable interrupts before any diagnostics, and a re-entrant fatal path stops silently instead of recursively printing. A fault inside the fatal exception handler likewise stops silently.\n- Full CPU register dumps in the fatal exception path are gated behind the `fatal-register-dump` cargo feature, which is off by default; `KERNEL EXCEPTION` reports the vector, error code, and RIP only.\n- The APIC MMIO pages (LAPIC and I/O APIC, from the MADT) are validated against the physical memory map (`MemoryMap::validate_mmio_page`) and against the kernel-image span before being mapped: they must not overlap `Usable`, `ExecutableAndModules`, or `BootloaderReclaimable` regions. A page that fails validation is not mapped, and the corresponding APIC route stays masked.\n- The frame allocator subtracts the kernel-image and boot-stack physical spans before accounting usable frames (`from_memory_map_excluding`).\n- Boot rejects a zero HHDM offset or one whose window covers the linked kernel image, and fails closed when the physical address width is unknown.\n- Allocation from interrupt/exception context is detected in debug builds (`INTERRUPT_DEPTH` tracking in `sync.rs`); I/O APIC redirection masking covers all architecturally possible entries, redirect-vector bounds are enforced, and the PIT unmask verifies the configured vector and mask state.\n- Context switches clear the direction flag; the IRQ stub stops instead of FXSAVEing through a null active-state pointer; the FXSAVE pointer is published with release semantics.\n- The trailing-`/` ENOTDIR semantics in the RAM filesystem were enforced, `cp`/`mv` move into an existing directory under the source's base name, `cat` maps control bytes to placeholders, and rejected non-printable input now rings the bell.\n- Release builds set `overflow-checks = true`.\n- Repo hygiene: tracked AI session transcripts and machine-local tool settings were untracked; CI pins `actions/checkout` to a commit SHA with `timeout-minutes` and a concurrency group; the Rust toolchain is pinned to an exact version.\n\nThese changes harden the current milestone; they do not establish any new isolation, SMP, or production-security claims.",
  "oldString": "Unsafe blocks should remain as small as practical and state the assumptions that make each operation valid. Avoid widening `unsafe impl` or introducing raw-pointer-based public APIs without a documented safety argument. The PS/2 routine consumes bytes from the controller, including discarding auxiliary/error bytes, so it must only be used by a single owner of that controller until input serialization is designed. External boot inputs, user pointers, MMIO, and DMA must not be treated as trusted merely because current boot data is trusted.",
  "path": "/home/ethan/rustix/docs/security.md"
}

    #[test]
    fn copy_and_move_into_an_existing_directory() {
        let mut fs = RamFs::new();
        let source = fs.create_file_path(ROOT_NODE, "/source.txt").unwrap();
        fs.write_file(source, b"data").unwrap();
        let dest_dir = fs.create_dir(ROOT_NODE, "destdir").unwrap();

        fs.copy_file_path(ROOT_NODE, "/source.txt", "/destdir")
            .unwrap();
        let copied = fs.resolve(dest_dir, "source.txt").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(fs.read_file(copied).unwrap(), b"data");
        // copy with a trailing separator also lands inside the directory
        fs.copy_file_path(ROOT_NODE, "/source.txt", "/destdir/")
            .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(fs.read_file(copied).unwrap(), b"data");

        // mv refuses to overwrite an existing target, even inside a directory
        fs.move_file_path(ROOT_NODE, "/source.txt", "/destdir/")
            .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(fs.resolve(ROOT_NODE, "/source.txt"), Err(FsError::NotFound));
        assert_eq!(
            fs.read_file(fs.resolve(dest_dir, "source.txt").unwrap())
                .unwrap(),
            b"data"
        );

        // mv source back onto itself is a no-op
        let source_again = fs.create_file_path(ROOT_NODE, "/source.txt").unwrap();
        fs.move_file_path(ROOT_NODE, "/source.txt", "/source.txt")
            .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(fs.kind(source_again), Ok(EntryKind::File));

use rustix_kernel::sync::InterruptContextGuard;

const EXCEPTION_COUNT: usize = 32;
const IRQ_FIRST_VECTOR: usize = 0x20;
const IRQ_LAST_VECTOR: usize = 0xef;
const IRQ_VECTOR_COUNT: usize = IRQ_LAST_VECTOR - IRQ_FIRST_VECTOR + 1;
const APIC_SPURIOUS_VECTOR: usize = 0xff;
/// Vectors 0xf0-0xfe have no registered source or IDT-worthy meaning today.
/// They are wired to a controlled fatal stop instead of left non-present.
const IRQ_UNWIRED_FIRST_VECTOR: usize = 0xf0;
const IRQ_UNWIRED_LAST_VECTOR: usize = 0xfe;
const IRQ_UNWIRED_COUNT: usize = IRQ_UNWIRED_LAST_VECTOR - IRQ_UNWIRED_FIRST_VECTOR + 1;
const IRQ_STUB_COUNT: usize = IRQ_VECTOR_COUNT + IRQ_UNWIRED_COUNT + 1;
const KERNEL_CODE_SELECTOR: u16 = 0x08;
const _: [(); 208] = [(); IRQ_VECTOR_COUNT];
const KERNEL_CODE_SELECTOR: u16 = 0x08;
const STACK_SIZE: usize = 16 * 1024;
const TSS_DESCRIPTOR_TYPE: u64 = 0x89;

#[repr(C, packed)]

```rust
unsafe fn mask_all_io_apic_entries(base: u64) -> Result<(), ApicError> {
    // Mask every architecturally possible redirection entry, not just the
    // hardware-reported count. Only the low register carries the mask bit; it
    // is addressable for all 120 possible entries (0x10 + 2 * 119 = 0xfe).
    let mut pin = 0u8;
    while pin < IO_APIC_MAX_REDIRECTIONS {
        let low = IO_APIC_REDIR_BASE
            .checked_add(pin.checked_mul(2).ok_or(ApicError::InvalidIoApicVersion)?)
            .ok_or(ApicError::InvalidIoApicVersion)?;
        if low > IO_APIC_REDIR_MAX {
            return Err(ApicError::InvalidIoApicVersion);
        }
        let current = unsafe { io_apic_read(base, low) };
        unsafe { io_apic_write(base, low, current | IO_APIC_REDIR_MASKED) };
        pin += 1;
    }
    Ok(())
}
```
For pin=119: low = 0x10 + 238 = 0xfe ≤ 0xfe ✓. This masks every entry without touching high registers ✓. Also removes reliance on redirection_registers for this path.

    fn all_threads_exited(&self) -> bool {
        self.thread_ids
            .iter()

            .all(|thread| self.policy.state(*thread) == Ok(ThreadState::Exited))
    }

    fn wake_expired(&mut self) {
        let now = self.ticks.ticks();
        for slot in 0..MAX_THREADS {
            if self.deadlines[slot].is_some_and(|deadline| now >= deadline) {
                self.deadlines[slot] = None;
                if self.policy.wake_one(self.timer_queues[slot]).is_err() {
                    THREAD_TEST_TRACE.store(99, Ordering::Release);
                }
            }
        }
    }

    fn wake_ready_events(&mut self) -> bool {
        self.wake_expired();
        let input_event = self.input_events_enabled
            && rustix_kernel::input::INPUT_EVENT_PENDING.swap(false, Ordering::AcqRel);
        if input_event && self.policy.wake_all(self.event_queue).is_err() {
            THREAD_TEST_TRACE.store(99, Ordering::Release);
        }
        input_event
    }

For the `-serial file:` comma issue: `serial_log.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix()` — if ROOT contains a comma, QEMU's chardev parsing breaks. Fix: use absolute path (no relative_to) and reject commas:

```python
    serial_log = TARGET_DIR / "rustix-qemu-serial.log"
    serial_log.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    serial_log.unlink(missing_ok=True)
    serial_path = serial_log.as_posix()
    if "," in serial_path or "=" in serial_path:
        raise BuildError("serial log path must not contain ',' or '=' characters: " + serial_path)
    serial_device = "file:" + serial_path
```
Wait — but with cwd=ROOT and relative path... using absolute is more robust with cwd unchanged? The command runs with cwd=ROOT presumably; absolute path works regardless ✓. Also note "-serial file:" with absolute path prints absolute path in log — harmless.

## Audit hardening (2026-11-16)

A full static security audit (boot trust boundary, allocators, paging, interrupts/scheduler, drivers, shell/VFS, build/CI) was performed and its actionable findings were applied. Highlights:

- The panic handler masks maskable interrupts before any diagnostics, and a re-entrant fatal path stops silently instead of recursively printing. A fault inside the fatal exception handler likewise stops silently.
- Full CPU register dumps in the fatal exception path are gated behind the `fatal-register-dump` cargo feature, which is off by default; `KERNEL EXCEPTION` reports the vector, error code, and RIP only.
- The APIC MMIO pages (LAPIC and I/O APIC, from the MADT) are validated against the physical memory map (`MemoryMap::validate_mmio_page`) and against the kernel-image span before being mapped: they must not overlap `Usable`, `ExecutableAndModules`, or `BootloaderReclaimable` regions. A page that fails validation is not mapped, and the corresponding APIC route stays masked.
- The frame allocator subtracts the kernel-image and boot-stack physical spans before accounting usable frames (`from_memory_map_excluding`).
- Boot rejects a zero HHDM offset or one whose window covers the linked kernel image, and fails closed when the physical address width is unknown.
- Allocation from interrupt/exception context is detected in debug builds (`INTERRUPT_DEPTH` tracking in `sync.rs`); I/O APIC redirection masking covers all architecturally possible entries, redirect-vector bounds are enforced, and the PIT unmask verifies the configured vector and mask state.
- Context switches clear the direction flag; the IRQ stub stops instead of FXSAVEing through a null active-state pointer; the FXSAVE pointer is published with release semantics.
- The trailing-`/` ENOTDIR semantics in the RAM filesystem were enforced, `cp`/`mv` move into an existing directory under the source's base name, `cat` maps control bytes to placeholders, and rejected non-printable input now rings the bell.
- Release builds set `overflow-checks = true`.
- Repo hygiene: tracked AI session transcripts and machine-local tool settings were untracked; CI pins `actions/checkout` to a commit SHA with `timeout-minutes` and a concurrency group; the Rust toolchain is pinned to an exact version.

These changes harden the current milestone; they do not establish any new isolation, SMP, or production-security claims.

## Security requirements for future subsystems

- Validate every physical range and checked address calculation before mapping or allocating it.
- Enforce least privilege in page mappings; avoid writable-executable pages by default.
- Keep user memory untrusted and use explicit copy-in/copy-out routines at syscall boundaries.
- Define ownership and lifetime rules for descriptors, processes, shared memory, and device buffers.
- Test malformed input and error paths as well as successful operation.
- Do not claim isolation or security guarantees until those properties have end-to-end tests and review.

## Assistant

_Thinking:_

The frame.rs diff looks correct. One subtle case worth double-checking: in the exclusion splitting loop, when `next_count < MAX_PIECES` guard fails for one side... can next exceed MAX_PIECES? Starting from 1 piece, each exclusion can add at most one net piece. pieces max = 1 + 7 = 8 = MAX_PIECES ✓ never exceeded. The `next_count < MAX_PIECES` conditions are pure belt-and-braces; if they ever clipped, that would silently drop a piece — which would leave a frame accounted as usable while NOT excluded → allocator could hand out a kernel-owned frame. Wait, no — dropping a piece means the bytes in the dropped piece are NOT allocated — the danger is the opposite: if a piece is dropped, those frames are just never allocated (leaked), not mis-owned. That's fail-closed ✓. But MAX_PIECES=8 with ≤7 exclusions never triggers it anyway. OK.

But hold on — there's a real edge: the `low half` clamp `region.start().max(LOW_MEMORY_RESERVED_END)` happens BEFORE exclusion subtraction. An exclusion below 1 MiB — pieces start at ≥ 1 MiB anyway ✓ no issue.

Another subtle case: exclusion fully covers a region piece → both branches false → piece dropped → 0 frames ✓ tested.

An exclusion that starts exactly at region_start: (s=region_start, e=ex_end): piece_start < exclusion_start false → left part dropped ✓; right part emitted ✓.

`exclusion_end <= piece_start || exclusion_start >= piece_end` → no overlap → keep piece ✓ (adjacent exclusion exactly touching piece boundaries → kept, correct: half-open).

_Thinking:_

interrupts.rs diff looks correct and consistent — the IDT loops cover 0x20..=0xef (existing), 0xf0..=0xfe (new), 0xff (spurious). The assembly emits 208 + 15 + 1 stubs, table has 224 entries, and all indices used in initialize_idt are < 224 ✓. One thing to double check: `rustix_irq_fatal_no_fx_state` is placed between `iretq` and `.section .rodata` — inside .text ✓ never fallen into ✓.

Wait — one issue: the new `rustix_irq_fatal_no_fx_state` label — is it needed `.global`? I added .global — harmless.

But hold on, a subtle correctness concern about the unwired vectors: `rustix_irq_dispatch` gets called for vectors 0xf0-0xfe from the stubs, and then `fatal_irq_halt()` is called for anything outside IRQ_FIRST..=IRQ_LAST. That path does NOT send an APIC EOI and does not restore FPU state — but it also never returns (machine halts), which is the intent ✓. Note: for 0xf0-0xfe, the stub FXSAVEs first (needs ACTIVE_FX_STATE_POINTER non-null — after boot init it is ✓).

Exception stubs: unchanged ✓.

One more check — the spurious vector IDT gate: vector 0xff via interrupt gate, IST 0, attributes 0x8e ✓ same as before.

+/// Set once the fatal panic path has started. A panic from inside the fatal
+/// path stops silently instead of recursively printing or looping diagnostics.
+static FATAL_PANIC_ACTIVE: core::sync::atomic::AtomicBool =
+    core::sync::atomic::AtomicBool::new(false);
+
 #[panic_handler]
 fn panic(info: &PanicInfo<'_>) -> ! {
+    // Mask maskable interrupts before any diagnostics run: another kernel
+    // thread must not resume after a panic is declared, and the fatal report
+    // must not be preempted mid-print.
+    //
+    // SAFETY: Kernel panic path runs at CPL 0; masking IF is always sound.
+    unsafe { core::arch::asm!("cli", options(nomem, nostack)) };
+    if FATAL_PANIC_ACTIVE.swap(true, core::sync::atomic::Ordering::Acquire) {

+            // SAFETY: Re-entrant panics are deliberately silent.
+            unsafe { core::arch::asm!("cli; hlt", options(nomem, nostack)) };

+/// Number of exception/IRQ entry frames currently active on this CPU.
+///
+/// Exception and device-IRQ entry paths increment this on entry and decrement
+/// it via [`InterruptContextGuard`] on the paths that return. The allocator
+/// reads it to refuse (in debug builds) allocations from interrupt context,
+/// where taking the heap lock could deadlock the CPU with interrupts masked.
+/// Not SMP-safe; single-vCPU bootstrap only.
+#[doc(hidden)]
+pub static INTERRUPT_DEPTH: AtomicUsize = AtomicUsize::new(0);
+
+/// Records one active exception/IRQ entry frame for the lifetime of the guard.
+///
+/// Fatal paths that never return simply leak their increment, which is the
+/// intended semantics: the machine is stopping. Not `Send`.
+#[doc(hidden)]
+pub struct InterruptContextGuard;
+
+impl InterruptContextGuard {
+    /// Mark the current exception/IRQ entry frame as active.
+    pub fn enter() -> Self {
+        INTERRUPT_DEPTH.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
+        Self
+    }
+}
+
+impl Drop for InterruptContextGuard {
+    fn drop(&mut self) {
+        INTERRUPT_DEPTH.fetch_sub(1, Ordering::Relaxed);
+    }
+}

All fixes are applied and the full validation suite passes. Here's the complete rundown.

## Validation actually observed (final tree)

- `just fmt` — clean
- `just test` — **136 host tests pass** (5 new)
- `just check` — clean, including with `--features fatal-register-dump`
- `just build` — release build with now-enabled `overflow-checks`
- `just verify` — BIOS + OVMF boots, active-map probe, thread/preemption smoke, UART/PS/2 input smoke — all pass
- `just fault-smoke` — page-fault and invalid-opcode injection both pass
- One real bug was caught by `just verify` during bring-up: my first mask-all-120 implementation rejected pin 119 (its high redirection register is 0xff, outside the in-tree `IO_APIC_REDIR_MAX` bound), which left the machine with IRQs disabled. Fixed to compute low registers only (0x10 + 2·pin ≤ 0xfe for all 120 entries); timer IRQ path now verified.

## What was applied

**Boot chain / memory**
- Kernel-image and boot-stack physical spans are now subtracted from usable frames (`FrameAllocator::from_memory_map_excluding`, `boot.rs` builds the exclusion list; new `InvalidExclusion` error; page-granular trimming of non-aligned exclusions)
- HHDM offset `0 == rejected`; HHDM window covering the linked kernel image rejected (verified compatible with the real Limine config: offset `0xffff800000000000`, image `0xffffffff80…`)
- Boot fails closed when CPUID hides the physical address width (`MissingPhysicalAddressBits`) instead of silently dropping all width checks
- MADT-derived LAPIC/I/O APIC MMIO pages must not overlap `Usable`/`ExecutableAndModules`/`BootloaderReclaimable` regions (new `MemoryMap::validate_mmio_page` / `ValidatedMmioSpan`) or the kernel image, before mapping. `map_mmio_page` now *requires* the span token (previously unenforced) — the "hostile firmware points I/O APIC at RAM" path is closed
- Release revisions noted via an accurate `NOTE:` diagnostic (the first version false-positived on Limine's revision 0 — fixed after observing the boot log)

**Interrupts / fatal path**
- Panic handler: `cli` first, plus `FATAL_PANIC_ACTIVE` recursion guard — re-entrant panic halts silently
- Exception dispatch: same recursion guard; register dumps gated behind `fatal-register-dump` (default off); `KERNEL EXCEPTION` keeps vector/error/RIP and CR2 reporting
- Vectors 0xf0–0xfe wired to a controlled fatal stop instead of non-present gates
- IRQ stub: null-guard on the FXSAVE destination (`rustix_irq_fatal_no_fx_state` loop); FX pointer now `AtomicPtr` + release semantics
- Context switch clears `DF` on resume (cooperative path)
- Heap: debug-build assert against allocation from interrupt/exception context (`INTERRUPT_DEPTH` via `InterruptContextGuard` in `sync.rs`), debug assert on arena/frame-backed dealloc failure
- Timer/event wake failure now prints and halts loudly instead of silently sleeping the shell thread

**Shell / VFS / drivers**
- Trailing-`/` operand demands a directory (ENOTDIR): `rm file/` no longer deletes; `cat`/`write`/`append` on `file/` error out; nonexistent `dest/` is an error
- `cp`/`mv` into an existing directory copy/move inside it under the source's base name; `mv` still never overwrites
- `append` pre-checks the cumulative 1024-byte bound before creating/mutating anything
- `cat` maps control bytes to `.`; non-printable input bytes ring the bell; `meminfo` null-checks its allocator pointer
- APIC: redirect vectors restricted to 0x20–0xef everywhere; PIT unmask verifies the programmed vector and masked state; masking covers all 120 architecturally possible redirection entries

**Repo / build / CI**
- Untracked the 8 tracked AI chat transcripts (29 MB), `.claude/settings*.json`, `.entire/settings.json`, `scripts/__pycache__/*.pyc`; `.gitignore` now covers `.agents/`, `.opencode/`, these files
- `actions/checkout` pinned to SHA `3d3c42e…` (v7.0.1), CI gets `timeout-minutes: 30` and a `concurrency` group; `rust-toolchain.toml` pins `1.99.1`; justfile uses `python3`; release profile sets `overflow-checks = true`; Limine hash provenance documented in `boot.py`; `-serial file:` uses an absolute path and rejects commas
- Docs updated: `docs/security.md` (audit-hardening section), `docs/architecture/{crabshell,memory}.md`

## Not applied, deliberately

- **`alloc_error_handler`** — the attribute is unstable on stable Rust (E0658); nothing links the `alloc` crate today. Documented in `docs/security.md` instead.
- **Allocation-authority singleton** for `FrameAllocator` — the caller-enforced contract stays; making it structural is an API redesign (e.g. consuming the `MemoryMap`), not a patch.
- **IST/exception-stack headroom for IRQ frames** (F1) and **IrqSpinLock same-context re-entrancy detection** (F5) — both need new mechanism (a second IST slot / per-lock owner token), not small fixes.
- **History rewrite** of the tracked transcripts — destructive; the untracking is staged but nothing was rewritten or committed. Committing is yours to do (`git commit` when ready).
- **KASLR** — stays off by explicit project decision; **PGP verification of the Limine `.sig`** — provenance documented in-tree, verification is a pin-bump-time step; **port-TOCTOU in the input-smoke TCP serial**, **keyboard/UART error-byte counters**, **serial `*overflow` surfacing** — all noted, low value relative to the risk of breaking a passing smoke suite.

## Risks / notes

- Nothing is committed; the deletions are staged, the rest is working-tree changes
- The `.agents/` untracking removes files from the index only — they remain in git history; purging requires a history rewrite and your explicit go-ahead
- Host tests plus QEMU (BIOS and OVMF) pass, but physical-hardware behavior is still unobserved — unchanged from the Milestone 3 status, which I did not alter
